## 🗂 What This Repo Is

This is a **single Next.js (App Router) repo** that contains two things:

1. **Ads for Good** — an agency website (the majority of `src/app/`)
2. **Chapter** — a lifecycle attribution and identity infrastructure platform built on top of Supabase + Next.js

They share one repo, one Vercel deployment. Be careful not to break agency pages when working on Chapter.

**Agency pages (DO NOT TOUCH unless explicitly asked):**
- `src/app/about`
- `src/app/contact`
- `src/app/for-businesses`
- `src/app/for-clients`
- `src/app/for-good`
- `src/app/for-people`
- `src/app/network`
- `src/app/page.tsx` (homepage — agency)

---

## 🏗 Chapter Architecture

### Stack
- **Frontend / API:** Next.js App Router (TypeScript)
- **Database:** Supabase (Postgres) — project ID `bvvmmhekdgskeilczeuy`, region `us-west-2`
- **Deployment:** Vercel

### Chapter API Endpoints (all in `src/app/api/`)
| Folder | Purpose |
|--------|---------|
| `pixel/` | Serves the client-side pixel script (pixel.js) |
| `chapter/collect` | Receives all pixel events → writes to `chapter_ingest.pixel_events` |
| `purchase/` | Server-side purchase ingestion → `chapter_ingest.purchase_events` |
| `conversion/` | Non-purchase conversions → `chapter_ingest.conversion_events` |
| `consent/` | Consent state tracking → `chapter_ingest.consent_events` |
| `identify/` | Identity stitching → `chapter_identity.identity_aliases` |
| `alias/` | Identity alias management |
| `offline/` | Offline milestones + identity seeds |
| `inquiry/` | Agency contact/lead form (NOT Chapter) |

### Pixel Files
- **`src/app/api/chapter/pixel.js/route.ts`** — serves the pixel.js script to clients (returns the JS as a string in the GET handler, `Cache-Control: no-store` so clients always pull the latest)
- **`src/app/api/pixel/route.ts`** — server-side ingest helper (NOT the script-serving file; CLAUDE.md previously documented this incorrectly)
- **`src/app/api/chapter/collect/route.ts`** (or similar) — receives events server-side

### Tier 1 first-party redirect (added June 10, 2026)
- **URL shape:** `https://ads4good.com/r/<client_key>/<slug>?<query>` — visitor 302s to a destination chosen by priority-ordered rules evaluated against identity/cart/geo/device/AB/time/query/referrer context. Click is logged to `chapter_ingest.pixel_events` as `event_name='redirect_click'` so it flows through canonical_v1 as a normal session entry. Identity + journey cookies are dropped so downstream pixel events stitch to the same identity. <50ms latency target.
- **Admin UI:** `/internal/redirect-rules` (gated by `CHAPTER_DASH_TOKEN` cookie). Create/edit/toggle/delete rules per (client_key, slug). Conditions are raw JSON (object = AND-ed predicates; empty `{}` = catch-all). Destination template supports `{q:utm_source}`, `{identity_key}`, `{client_key}`, `{country}`, `{region}`, `{city}`, `{device_type}`, `{os}`.
- **Route handler:** `src/app/r/[client_key]/[slug]/route.ts`. Library modules in `src/app/lib/redirect/` (rules, identity, geo, device, ab, cart, segments, conditions, click-logger, template, consent).
- **Consent gate is always-on.** `chapter_consent` cookie on the redirect apex; `opt_out` blocks the click log + cookie issuance but still 302s. No per-client toggle (matches the `/api/chapter/collect` contract). Per-client knob is the "default when cookie is absent" — v1 hardcoded `"opt_in"`.
- **Operator guide:** `docs/tier1-redirect.md` (URL shape, all 17 condition types, template vars, example SQL, ESP wrap patterns for Mailchimp/Klaviyo/Shopify Email/Meta/Google Ads).

## 🗄 Supabase Schema Overview

### Data Flow
```
chapter_ingest (raw events)
   ↓
chapter_identity (identity resolution)
   ↓
chapter_journey (session grouping)
   ↓
chapter_model (unified events + lifecycle chapters)
   ↓
chapter_attribution (paths + attribution + reporting)
   ↓
chapter_reporting (dashboard outputs — EOS-specific for now)
```

### Schemas
| Schema | Purpose |
|--------|---------|
| `chapter_ingest` | Raw event intake — pixel, purchase, conversion, offline, consent |
| `chapter_identity` | Identity graph — aliases, canonical resolution, links |
| `chapter_journey` | Session/journey containers |
| `chapter_model` | Unified event stream, timeline, lifecycle chapters |
| `chapter_attribution` | Channel paths, attribution models (first/last/linear) |
| `chapter_reporting` | Dashboard-ready outputs (generalizing per-tile; new tables drop the `eos_` prefix) |
| `chapter_analysis` | Experimental, QA, debug objects — not production |
| `chapter_config` | Per-client config — `clients` (PK config: storefront_domain / boundary_event_name / display_tz / email_source_patterns + agency_key added June 18), `client_secrets`, `shopify_webhook_secrets`, `square_webhook_secrets` + `square_oauth_tokens` (added June 9), `email_campaigns`, `email_engagement_events`, `connections_cohorts` + `connections_cohort_members`, `redirect_rules` + `redirect_ab_experiments` (added June 10, Tier 1 redirect), `users` (Sprint 5a auth + agency_key column added June 18), `agencies` (Sprint 7, June 18), `allowed_email_domains` (Sprint 7, June 18), `tracking_ignore_list` (June 17) |
| `chapter_observations` | Observations engine — `questions` catalog, `findings` state machine, `runs` audit (added June 2, 2026) |
| `chapter_audit` | Auth-attempt + PII-view audit logs (added Fix #26 part 4) |
| `chapter_recommendations` | Recommendations engine — `rules` config, `findings` state machine, `runs` audit (added June 14, 2026) |
| `chapter_inquiries` | Sprint 8 inquiries — `threads` + `messages` + auto-bump trigger. Per-client inquiry threads; chapter_staff reply via `/internal/inbox`; clients/agencies see status updates via `/chapter/<key>/inbox`. Gchat webhook on new thread + client reply (chapter_staff suppressed). Storage bucket `inquiry-attachments` (private, 10MB cap, image-only). RLS-on-no-policies; access enforced server-side. |
| `crm` | Internal CRM — `prospects`, `communications` (manual touchpoints + n8n-synced emails/meetings), `interactions` (observed signals — site visits / GBP / Yelp / inquiry / review / referral), `clients` (converted prospects → paying clients). RLS enabled, service_role full grants (June 18 added INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on prospects/communications/interactions which were SELECT-only). `/internal/crm` page operates over this schema. See "Agency dogfood" entry under Completed Fixes for the full CRM↔Chapter integration architecture. |
| `tasks` | n8n-driven internal task tracker — `task_batches` (per-email batches from outgoing project updates), `tasks` (individual action items per batch). `/internal/tasks` admin UI. |

### Critical Tables
- `chapter_ingest.pixel_events` — primary behavioral feed
- `chapter_ingest.purchase_events` — primary revenue signal (contains `raw` Shopify JSON with `browser_ip`)
- `chapter_ingest.conversion_events` — non-purchase conversions
- `chapter_ingest.offline_milestones` — offline event feed
- `chapter_ingest.offline_identity_seeds` — CRM/offline identity bridge
- `chapter_identity.identity_aliases` — core identity graph edges
- `chapter_identity.identity_canon` — fast canonical identity lookup
- `chapter_identity.identity_canonical` — recursive resolution engine (VIEW)
- `chapter_identity.identity_links` — journey ↔ identity bridge
- `chapter_journey.journeys` — session containers (has `country`, `city`, `user_agent`, `region`)
- `chapter_model.lifecycle_chapters` — attribution boundary events (VIEW)
- `chapter_model.unified_events_v2` — canonical event stream (VIEW)

## 📊 Chapter Dashboard (agency-operator surface)

**URL:** `/chapter/*` — agency-operator dashboard, sees all clients via top-of-sidebar switcher. **First-paint default:** `/chapter/observations`.

**Future:** `/chapter/[client_key]/*` — client-scoped surface, one dashboard per client_key with scoped auth. **Deferred** until agency surface is data-wired. Today's URL/code structure is compatible (page components read `client_key` from context, not hardcoded) so adding the route group later is additive. Constraint: `client_key` always contains an underscore by convention (e.g. `eos_fabrics`, `projectagram_reels`, future `lift_agency`) so it can never collide with the static page slugs (`observations`, `overview`, etc.).

### Auth (stopgap until Supabase auth)
- Env var `CHAPTER_DASH_TOKEN` (random secret) compared verbatim against a `chapter_auth` cookie set by `POST /api/chapter-auth`
- Middleware at root `middleware.ts` gates `/chapter/*` except `/chapter/login` + `/api/chapter-auth`
- 14-day cookie session; fail-closed if env var is unset (returns 503)
- **Migration path:** when Supabase auth is wired, the cookie+middleware logic gets replaced by Supabase's middleware helpers; UI for login swaps; rest of the app reads `user` from server-side auth instead of cookie.

### File layout
- `src/app/chapter/layout.tsx` — minimal pass-through (lets login render without chrome)
- `src/app/chapter/(authed)/layout.tsx` — sidebar + topbar chrome + ChapterProvider + PinnedObservation
- `src/app/chapter/(authed)/<page>/page.tsx` — one per nav item (8 pages: observations, overview, channels, paths, lift, attribution, journeys, raw)
- `src/app/chapter/login/page.tsx` — password form
- `src/app/chapter/chapter.css` — design tokens scoped to `.chapter-app` wrapper so they cannot leak into agency Tailwind
- `src/app/chapter/_components/*` — 13 reusable primitives (Icon, Move, Lcm, Dropdown, RoleBar, PathRender, ChannelChip, Sidebar, TopBar, KpiStrip, PinnedObservation, ChapterContext, mockdata, format)
- `src/app/api/chapter-auth/route.ts` — login POST handler

### Data fetching pattern (locked May 15, 2026)
- Dashboard is a **trusted agency-operator surface** → queries use `service_role` with explicit `WHERE client_key = $selected` in every WHERE clause
- NOT `chapter_app` + `SET ROLE` — that's the ingest-layer defense-in-depth (Fix #26 Part 2). Dashboard's defense-in-depth is the password gate.
- Per-page split:
  - `<page>/page.tsx` — **server component**, reads `searchParams`, runs Supabase queries, passes data + params via props
  - `<page>/<Page>Client.tsx` — **client component**, holds interactive state (filters/drawers), receives data via props
- Each existing all-`"use client"` page gets split during its wiring session; ~20–40 LOC of refactor per page (no new logic, just relocation).

### State persistence (URL query params)
- **Selected client** → `?client=eos_fabrics` (fallback to cookie, fallback to first client in CLIENTS list)
- **Date range** → `?range=30d` (other accepted values: `7d` / `14d` / `90d` / `mtd` / `qtd` / `ytd` / custom-as-ISO-pair)
- **Compare mode** → `?compare=prior|yoy|none`
- **Attribution model** → `?model=first|last|linear|custom`
- Server components read from `searchParams` in `page.tsx`. Client components read same via `useSearchParams()`. Updates write back via `router.replace()` (no history pollution per click).
- Middleware preserves the full path + query string when redirecting unauthenticated users to login, so a bookmarked URL like `/chapter/raw?client=eos_fabrics&range=90d` survives the auth round-trip.
- Rationale: shareable/bookmarkable URLs; server-side fetching gets params natively; fewer client-state syncing bugs than a separate global store.

### Reporting generalization strategy (decided May 14, 2026)
- New tables live in `chapter_reporting.*` **alongside** existing `eos_*` tables — no schema rename, no big-bang migration.
- Naming: drop the `eos_` prefix. `chapter_reporting.eos_traffic_overview_snapshot` → `chapter_reporting.traffic_overview_snapshot_v1`.
- **Per-tile recipe** when wiring a dashboard tile:
  1. If an `eos_*` analog exists, use its loader SQL as the template
  2. Strip the `client_key = 'eos_fabrics'` filter + any other EOS hardcoding
  3. Add `client_key` to the output column list if missing
  4. Add an index on `(client_key, ...)` per the multi-tenant pattern (Fix #26 Part 1)
  5. Run the new loader populating all active clients
  6. **Verify the new table against the existing `eos_*` equivalent** — `WHERE client_key='eos_fabrics'` row counts + values match
  7. **Also verify against an external truth source** — Shopify Admin reports, Google Analytics, the platform's own analytics — to catch logic the `eos_*` loader inherited that may be quietly wrong (or that may diverge by definition; document divergences)
  8. Wire the dashboard tile to the new generic table
- Old `eos_*` tables remain live indefinitely. No drop until we've operated for ~weeks on the new ones with confidence (cleanup is a future session).
- **Scope discipline:** only generalize tables the dashboard actually consumes. The 51-object `chapter_reporting` set (audit `chapter-scripts/audit-chapter-reporting.js`) has many intermediate/deprecated entries. Don't touch what's not consumed.

### Tier-based feature stripping
- All clients see the full feature set in v1. When tier-based stripping is needed, gate sections/tiles by `client.tier` (`Starter` / `Mid` / `Top`). The sidebar already shows a lock pill for Starter on the Observations item — extend this pattern to per-page lockouts rather than designing for tier-stripping from scratch.

### Mobile responsiveness
- Sidebar collapses to off-canvas drawer (hamburger toggle in topbar) below 1024px
- KPI strip → 2 cols on phone; lifecycle hero metrics same
- Tables: horizontal scroll inside their card, never the page
- Top bar: title row + dropdown row stack; subtitle clamped to 1 line; "Compare" dropdown hidden on phone
- `/chapter/journeys`: detail panel **hidden on phone** (event timeline + sub-grids don't render usefully on small screens). Identity list is the mobile view.
- `.chapter-app { overflow-x: hidden; max-width: 100vw }` is the hard guarantee against dark-body bleed-through from any rogue child element.

## ✅ Completed Fixes (as of August 4, 2026)

### Lagged Impact page review — two tabs + header tooltips + Ranked CI column (Aug 6, 2026)
- **Split into two URL-driven tabs (`?view=ranked|explore`, default ranked).** **Ranked pairs** (landing) = the pre-baked LI3 discovery table (every pairing Chapter surfaces, ranked by lagged return-lift). **Explore a pair** = the Channel A/B pickers + swap + Treatment/Lookforward + the per-pair lag table + "show your work" series chart. Tab bar under the shared hero + data-depth caveat; hero/caveat/foot shared across both. **Clicking a ranked row jumps into Explore** anchored on that pair (`selectPair` sets `channel_a`/`channel_b` + `view=explore` in one nav). Implemented by wrapping each existing block in a `{view === … && (…)}` conditional (no DOM reorder), so picker→Explore, ranked table→Ranked, pair card→Explore. Shareable + survives the pair-select navigation.
- **Header tooltips → instant navy info box on BOTH tables.** This page's `HeaderCell` gained a `title` prop + the same instant `#1F2D43` callout used elsewhere (was plain). Every column on the Ranked + Explore headers now has a plain-language definition (Treated/Baseline rate, Abs/Rel lift, 95% CI, Status, Lag, Channel pair).
- **Added the "95% CI (abs diff)" column to the Ranked table.** It was absent because `lagged_impact_ranked` returns point estimates + gate status but not the CI *bounds* (it computes `se_diff` internally only to set the gate). Verified via `pg_get_functiondef` that `lagged_impact_ranked` + `lagged_impact_pair` use the IDENTICAL CI math (`se = √(p_t(1−p_t)/n_t + p_b(1−p_b)/n_b)`, ±1.96·se). Since the ranked row already returns all four counts, computed the exact CI **client-side** (`absLiftCiPp`) — no DB migration, reconciles byte-for-byte with the per-pair drill-in. Added to `RANKED_GRID` (+ wider min-width) rendered `[+X.Xpp → +Y.Ypp]` like Explore.
- **Files:** `connections/lagged-impact/LaggedImpactClient.tsx` only. No DB changes. Also escaped pre-existing unescaped quotes/apostrophes in the hero paragraph (lint-clean).
- **Follow-ups (same review):**
  - **95% CI column relabeled** `95% CI` / `Lift (PP)` (was the jargon-y "(abs diff)") on both tables.
  - **Frozen header** on the Ranked table — wrapped in a `overflow:auto; max-height:70vh` scroll region with the header row `position:sticky; top:0` + opaque fill, so it stays visible while scrolling long pair lists (kept horizontal scroll too). Explore table (≤5 rows) left alone.
  - **All-time treatment window + lag selector (methodology change, operator-approved).** Verified on EOS that widening the treatment window from the old ⅓-of-range slice to **all available history** nearly doubles confident pairs (5→9 at 30d lag; 15 at 7d, 4 at 90d — longer lag eats treatment room via the RPC's `eff_end = last−lag` right-censoring). Rewrote `page.tsx`: treatment window = client's full `session_channel_entries_v1` span (new `cachedClientDataSpan` helper — two indexed min/max reads, no new RPC), dropped the ⅓–⅔ split (the RPC's censoring already guarantees lookforward room), Explore runs every lag `< spanDays`, series spans all-time. Added a **Lag window selector** (`?ranked_lag_days=`, default 30d) in the Ranked card head — the discovery table now re-ranks at the chosen lag. The top calendar Range no longer drives this page (only the CSI cross-link). Hero copy + Explore treatment readout rewritten to match. **Seasonality note:** all-time pooling ignores trend — fine at EOS's 128-day depth; for long-history (~18mo) clients we'll add a history selector when it matters.
  - **Files:** `connections/lagged-impact/page.tsx` (rewrite), `LaggedImpactClient.tsx` (props + hero + picker readout + Lag selector), `_lib/dashboard-rpc.ts` (`cachedClientDataSpan`).

### Cross-Source Influence page review — hero rewrite + filter grouping + anchor-window alignment (Aug 6, 2026)
- **Tab-by-tab review continued onto `/chapter/connections/influence`.** Operator-driven, batched-and-held, then committed together. All in the named influence files; typecheck clean.
- **1 — "How this page works" (hero left) → bullet list.** Replaced the dense paragraph with 5 bullets (Choose an Anchor → identifies session-entry channel; middle column = identities; L&R panels = connections; Left = touched N days *before*; Right = touched N days *after*) + the closing "describes connections, not cause · measured cousin lives at Lagged Impact." "N days" resolves live to the selected `windowDays`.
- **2 — "About this anchor" (hero right) → "How To Interpret", dynamic bullets.** Rewrote `AnchorExplanation` to take BOTH `upstreamTop` + `downstreamTop` (was a single `exampleRow`) and render a live worked example: **Anchor: {label}.** {nAnchor} identities {entered via/viewed/clicked} {label} · **Left:** {n} people {verb} {conn} N days before the {label} touch · **Right:** … N days after. Verbs adapt per anchor type + per connection type; falls back to "no connections above the sample floor yet" when a side panel is empty. Dropped the old per-anchor-type definition/reading prose + now-unused props (campaignUniqueClickers, cohortKind/Uploaded/Matched, matchRate, connectionsNoun, exampleRow/Dir).
- **3 — Lag/Outcome window definitions, side by side** under the bullets on a thin divider: **Lag Window · {N}d** — "Timeframe of Left & Right panel you want to see" · **Outcome Window · {N}d** — "Timeframe after a Connection to count a purchase." Current live values appended as accent chips.
- **4 — Filter-bar clustered into 3 labeled, bracketed groups** via a new `FilterGroup` helper (uppercase label + `⎣___⎦` bottom bracket in `--ink-4` @ 50%): **Anchor Filters** (type toggle + anchor-value dropdown), **Upstream / Downstream Connection Filters** (the All/Channels/Pages/Campaigns connection-view toggle), **Timing Windows** (Lag + Outcome dropdowns — grouped so the row stays balanced). Bar switched to `align-items: flex-start`; the standalone "Anchor window: {range}" readout nudged down (`marginTop`) to sit on the control-row baseline.
- **5 — Anchor-window default aligned to the top Date Range selector.** The "Anchor window" readout prints `{range}` (driven by `?range=`, written by `setDateRange`), but this page hardcoded its own `DEFAULT_RANGE = "90d"` while the global TopBar/context default is `"30d"` — so on first load the top showed "Last 30 days" while the anchor window silently used 90d and the readout disagreed. Set `DEFAULT_RANGE = "30d"` so they match on first load and track together on change. Trade-off: smaller anchor pop at 30d gates a few more connection rows to "below sample floor"; bump via the top selector for the wider 90d sample.
- **Files:** `connections/influence/InfluenceClient.tsx` (hero bullets, `AnchorExplanation` rewrite, `FilterGroup` + clustering), `connections/influence/page.tsx` (`DEFAULT_RANGE`). No DB changes.
- **Follow-ups (same review, second commit):**
  - **Caveat footer full-width + centered.** Moved "This describes connections… does not estimate cause / Lagged Impact" out of the left column into a full-width, center-justified footer on a thin divider — balances the two columns and shortens the hero banner.
  - **Timing group tidied.** Merged the standalone right-pushed "Anchor window: {range}" readout INTO the Timing group (right of Outcome, inside the bracket), deleting the awkward `marginLeft:auto` gap; `justify-content: center` on the whole filter bar so the three bracketed groups center as a row; renamed "Timing Windows" → **"Timing Window Filters"**.
  - **Header tooltips → navy Chapter info box.** `HeaderCell`'s delayed gray native `title` replaced with a custom instant tooltip matching the Lifecycle Overview chart callout (`#1F2D43` fill, white text, rounded, shadow, upward arrow, 240px wrap, uppercase/letter-spacing reset). Shared by both Upstream + Downstream panels.
- **Filter-grouping REVERTED (third commit, operator disliked it).** The `FilterGroup` label+bracket clustering was pulled back out — all filters (anchor-type toggle, anchor dropdown, connection-view toggle, Lag/Outcome dropdowns) return to a single left-aligned wrapping row. `FilterGroup` component deleted. "Anchor window: {range}" readout stacked (label over right-aligned value) at the row's right end. **Breadcrumb / "You came from" trail fully removed** — UI block + the `?bc=` tracking machinery (trail state, bc-writing in `rehomeOn`, `rehomeToBreadcrumb`/`clearTrail`, encode/decode/label helpers); row-click still re-anchors, just no trail. Escaped a pre-existing unescaped apostrophe in the page subtitle (was lint-latent).

### Lifecycle Overview page review — 8 UX/data fixes (Aug 6, 2026)
- **Tab-by-tab review continued onto the Lifecycle Overview page** (`/chapter/overview`). Operator drove one-question-at-a-time; edits batched and held per "don't ship after every edit" instruction, then committed together. The Multi-channel rename shipped separately just before the hold (`baee2e8`); the other 7 land in this commit. All in the named Overview files + 2 shared primitives; agency-site WIP untouched; typecheck clean.

- **2 — New "Journey completeness" tile.** Reads `chapter_reporting.journey_completeness(client, start, end)` (RPC applied via MCP) = % of KNOWN customers whose sessions Chapter stitched across visits. Foot: "of N known customers stitched across sessions", `good: true`, prior-window delta via `ppDelta`. This is the shippable companion to identification rate; the **naive anon→anon stitch-rate metric was deliberately held** (denominator dominated by one-time browsers; can't distinguish genuine one-timers from cookie-cleared returners — revisit when 1P durability matures).

"View all" → recommendations; empty state "No active recommendations right now — all clear." Dropped the mock-preview disclaimer + the now-unused OBSERVATIONS import. Removed the native `title` entirely, defaulted `showTooltip=true` (so no usage can silently regress), and the custom tooltip renders on `mouseenter` = instant. Restyled `.role-tip` from dark navy (`var(--ink)`) to the **Chapter orange callout** (`var(--accent)` fill, white text, orange pointer arrow + shadow); dropped the redundant color swatch. Also upgrades the Channel Roles page (already used the custom tooltip) to the new styling for free.

### #2 L/I/V fuller review + 2 fixes (Aug 6, 2026)
- **The un-reviewed L/I/V surfaces** (the Aug-3/4 audit only covered default-view cards + the matrix). Reviewed each vs live EOS. Two clean, two bugs:
  - **Incrementality value_band axis — CLEAN.** conv rate correctly null (value buckets derive from purchase amount → non-converters unbucketable → conv-rate intentionally hidden; `have_conv_data` guard prevents NaN); tiny-n channels gated (`gateState` <30 → hidden). Design-note present.
  - **Incrementality location axis — WAS BROKEN → FIXED.** The live RPC runs **74–131s** (per-identity region-resolution join over all journeys; value_band by contrast is 112ms) — it exceeded the dashboard PostgREST timeout, the wrapper caught the error and returned `[]`, so the **location cohort tab silently showed "No channels detected."** Confirmed via `pg_stat_statements` (mean 131,155ms). **Fix (option A, mirrors Sprint 9's subscriber-axis snapshot):** populate `incrementality_snapshot_v1` for `cohort_axis IN ('location','value_band')` in `refresh-derived-snapshots` (bumped that cron's `maxDuration` 300→800 for the slow location population — direct connection, 10-min statement_timeout, so the DB isn't the constraint, wall-clock is), and generalized `incrementalitySnapshotLookup` to serve ANY axis from the snapshot at the default 90d window (live fallback otherwise). **Lands when the derived-snapshots cron next runs (04:25 UTC) or on manual trigger** — until then location still falls back to the slow live RPC. Commit `83c2619`.
  - **Contribution — no low-presence floor → FIXED.** Channels touching <20 chapters (EOS organic social =7, paid social =1) got full cards + quadrant verdicts from single-bucket noise, AND their noisy incrementals polluted the **median split** that quadrants the real channels. Added `LOW_PRESENCE_FLOOR=20` (mirrors CR1's `LOW_SAMPLE_N`): below-floor channels are excluded from the median (both card-compute + matrix), forced to `unscored`, and rendered faded with a "Low sample" pill + honest headline. Modest for EOS today (its median is unaffected) but a real robustness gap for other clients/windows. Same commit.
  - **CR2 (two-period comparison) — SOUND.** both-confident gate, graceful missing-prior handling (`&& priorRow` guard, no crash for young clients), population-shift caveat present.
- **Method note:** the location-axis bug is the same class as the pre-Sprint-9 cliff — a heavy RPC silently exceeding the timeout and rendering a feature empty. `pg_stat_statements` mean_exec_time is the fastest way to catch these (131s jumped right out).

### Session state snapshot + EOS organic-search/SEO case + open threads (Aug 6, 2026)
- **Written before a machine restart to guarantee continuity.** Git is clean + pushed (latest `26dfd03`). Everything below + the entries under it is the durable record of the Aug 5–6 sessions.
- **Observations FULLY retired from nav (commits `b4eea73` + `3e2d5f9`).** Was staff-only since July 31; now removed from the sidebar for everyone (incl. chapter_staff), the `isStaff` gate deleted, and BOTH `/chapter/observations` AND the `/chapter` root redirect now point to `/chapter/recommendations` (root was still routing through the retired page — a leftover). `ObservationsClient` + the `chapter_observations` schema still exist (frozen) for a future hard-delete. The engine cron was already removed from `vercel.json` (last clean run Aug 5 05:00 UTC).
- **EOS organic-search → SEO investment case (verified, hand this to the EOS conversation).** Grew out of verifying R2.5. All numbers checked against live data:
  - Organic search is EOS's **#1 new-customer channel** and genuinely **incremental** (Incrementality tab: +135% conversion lift in non-subscribers, not just correlation).
  - It **closes cold, decisively**: 60% of its new customers buy on their **first visit**, 76% within two — verified as rich full-funnel sessions (median 228 events, land→browse→cart→buy), NOT thin/stitching-gap artifacts (0% thin vs NSC direct's 42%).
  - **~90% are genuinely first-time buyers** — cross-checked against Shopify's own customer IDs over EOS's full **2014-onward** order history (2,036 orders; NOT just since tracking). ~10% are returning buyers Chapter couldn't stitch (the identity-durability gap the 1P work is closing).
  - **Visit distribution** (organic-entry new customers): 60% one visit · 16% two · then a tail out to 15 visits. Returners (20 of 50) come back **overwhelmingly via organic search + direct (80%)**; email/referral only in the deep-research tail; **zero paid** (EOS runs no paid).
  - **The pitch:** organic search is EOS's best-proven, incremental, zero-media-cost acquisition engine, carrying both acquisition AND the return loop largely solo → **invest in SEO** (an ads-for-Good service). The kicker most agencies can't offer: **Chapter ties organic entry → first purchase at the identity level, so SEO ROI is provable over time.** Honest guardrail for the pitch: today's 60% single-touch reflects high-intent self-selected searchers; incremental SEO into broader/informational queries won't convert at that rate — scope it to the product/category/high-intent queries already winning, treat top-funnel content as a separate longer-payback bet. "New" = new buyer (not visitor); ~10% haircut for the stitching miss. Same flywheel as R1.5→Chapter Links: recommendation → ads-for-Good service → Chapter measures it.

Trigger it manually (needs `CRON_SECRET`, after deploy) OR it self-corrects Monday. R1.3 false alarms already cleared.

Chapter gets the IP at the Vercel edge but discards it; derive a hosting/datacenter-ASN flag at ingest + feed v1. Real ingest change, billing-adjacent — wants the operator's eyes, not a solo build.

### Health check (green) + Recommendation-rule verification — R1.3 bug found + fixes (Aug 6, 2026)
- **#3 nightly-chain health check — ALL GREEN.** Swept `_snapshot_runs` (attribution chain / journey_resolved / purchase_summary), the 3 dashboard MVs, session_channel_entries_v1, global snapshots, usage_snapshot, and the Recommendations engine runs. Every target `ok`, all real clients fresh (MVs ~11h from the 03:00 UTC refresh, derived ~9.6h from 04:25, usage today), zero stuck runs, all 6 clients' rec runs `ok`. **Only blemish:** 8 orphan `_snapshot_runs` rows for the two hard-deleted July-30 signup-bombing junk tenants (`afjyccmwexwaozek_01` / `jvuhsqvxemukffgxkhnluol_01`) — cosmetic (they don't reach the daily-digest, which only watches active clients). Cleaned up.
- **#1 Recommendation-rule verification — verified all 11 active findings across EOS/NSC/adsforgood against live data.** Results:
  - **✅ Accurate:** R1.1 bot-rate (58%/74%/39% — exact match, correctly low-severity); R2.5-EOS organic (deep-verified earlier: rich sessions, ~90% truly-new buyers); R1.5-EOS 61% direct (real).
  - **⚠️ R5.4-EOS stale (not a bug):** card showed old v1 `210/$22,415`; RPC now returns correct v2 `257/1,125/23%/$26,842`. Frozen from the run just before the Aug-5 v2 repoint → **refreshes on the next engine run** (no code change needed — the RPC is already v2). To refresh immediately, trigger the `refresh-recommendations` cron after the deploy lands; otherwise self-corrects Monday.
  - **⚠️ R2.5-NSC coverage-gap inflation:** "Direct wins 85% single-touch" (HIGH/strong) — but **42% of those closes are thin sessions** (≤2 events, no page_view; the Book Now→Square cross-domain flow dropping pre-booking browsing) vs EOS organic's 0% thin. The rule's hedge + "confirm pixel coverage" prompt point there, so not wrong, but HIGH+strong overstates it. Fix: thin-session guard (below).
  - **❌ R1.3 REAL BUG (both EOS + NSC, HIGH-severity false alarm):** the metric never measured identity stitching. Code (`R1_3.ts:102`) computed `(non-bot multi-event journeys) / (ALL journeys)` — denominator had **no bot filter** — which equals `100 − bot_share` (EOS: 26% "stitched" ≈ 100−74% bot). The claimed "42%→26% stitching drop, aliases failing to link, repeat customers look new" was **entirely the Aug meta-crawler bot surge + traffic-mix shift**, not alias-linking. Two deeper problems: filtering the denominator doesn't help (`bot_likely` is 0.3%; the crawler hides in the `suspect` bucket, which counts as non-bot), and the code comment falsely claimed `journey_resolved_v1.canonical_identity_key` exists (it doesn't — so R1.3 never had the canonical to measure stitching, hence the bad proxy).
    - **FIX (don't disable — redefine):** new `chapter_reporting.rec_r1_3_stitch_coverage` RPC = **known-identity rate among non-bot ENGAGED journeys** (canonical via `journeys.last_identity_key → identity_canon`, prefix `email_sha256:`/`*customer_id:`). Bot-robust (num+denom both non-bot-engaged). Validated: EOS 0.5%→0.9%, NSC 4.5%→15.5% — real resolution **improved** (the 1P/cart-token/`_shopify_y` work), so R1.3 correctly stops firing. Added a base-floor guard (structural low-identification ecom like EOS at ~1% shouldn't alarm on noise) + reframed the story to "identity-resolution coverage of engaged traffic," not "aliases failing to link." The 2 stale wrong R1.3 findings were manually resolved (state='resolved') so the misleading HIGH cards clear immediately, not just on the next run (the fixed rule won't re-fire them — EOS is below the materiality floor and both clients' resolution improved). NSC's R2.5 (HIGH→low + caveat) refreshes on the next engine run.
- **Method note:** verification-before-trust caught a HIGH-severity client-facing false alarm (R1.3) that had been firing for two clients — the single highest-value catch of the review. Same discipline as the CSI6 / Recommendations-"shells" / M1 wins.
- **Goal:** validate the bot classifier `journey_bot_classification_v1` (the billing metric) against ground truth without hand-labeling, using EOS's Blockify (Shopify bot-blocker) reporting as an independent second opinion. Analyzed the 30d "allowed" (1,518 rows) + "blocked" (921 rows) visitor-log xlsx exports (openpyxl streaming; scripts in scratchpad). **Read-only; no code shipped — this is a finding + a doc correction + a queued fix direction.**
- **Blockify can NOT serve as calibration ground-truth (original plan dead).** It's a **geo/IP blocklist, not a behavioral human-verifier**: blocked reasons are 678 "Blocked by Country" + 242 "Blocked by IP range" + 1 by UA (blocks Singapore/China datacenter farms — Tencent/Alibaba/Huawei Cloud). Its "allowed" set isn't clean-human either — **~42% on datacenter/CDN ISPs** (Cloudflare 415, Microsoft 86, Fastly 68, Google 37, Amazon 25 of 1,518) + **43% flagged VPN/Proxy**. And it **under-logs ~10×**: allowed+blocked `Total access` sums to ~2,891 visits/30d, but EOS's ~370 orders imply ~15k human sessions — 1,518 "allowed" would be a ~24% conversion rate (impossible), so the export is a filtered subset of evaluated/flagged visitors, not a census → its rates can't benchmark Chapter's ~73% exclusion. **No per-row join either** — Chapter deliberately stores no IP (GDPR), and Blockify's UA is only 36 coarse families ("chrome/desktop"), useless for matching.
- **But it exposed a real, Chapter-confirmed v1 blind spot (the valuable output).** v1 scores purely on **on-site behavior** (event count, duration) — it never sees IP/ISP, so a datacenter bot that executes JS + fires several events reads as `human_likely`. Chapter's own data confirms it: v1 bills as `human_likely` multi-event (the billed proxy) **~1,138 journeys/30d from Blockify's exact blocked geos — China 637, Vietnam 233, Singapore 166, Hong Kong 102** (~8% of ~14,050 billed human; likely ~12–15% including other bot-farm origins BD/ID/UA/AR/MY/TR in the same tail). Almost certainly datacenter bots (multi-event but from cloud regions EOS doesn't sell to).
- **Impact:** NOT a live billing bug (EOS is operator-billed flat, not on a verified-journey tier), but (a) it will inflate the billable "human" count when tiers go live, and (b) it dilutes EOS's human analytics (conversion/attribution) with non-converting bot journeys.
- **Fix direction (queued, not built):** Chapter already receives the IP at the Vercel edge (it derives `country` from it) but discards it and never derives ISP/ASN. Add a **datacenter-ASN / hosting-provider flag at ingest** (the dimension Blockify uses via VPN/Proxy + ISP) and feed it into v1 — the one bot class v1 is structurally blind to. Interim crude version: down-weight/exclude multi-event journeys from datacenter-bot geos with zero purchase history. **This reframes the classifier-calibration item: the highest-value move is the ASN dimension, not hand-labeling the behavioral boundary.** The `/internal/classifier-calibration` labeling tool is still at **0 labels** (never run); it remains available but is now secondary to the ASN work.
- **Doc correction (important — the July-27 entry is loose/wrong):** billing counts **`human_likely` ONLY**, NOT `human_likely + suspect`. Verified in code: `BillingClient` (`Real = human_likely; excluded = suspect + bot`) + `refresh-usage-snapshot` cron (utilization = `human_likely / ceiling`). So billing is **conservative** (excludes all `suspect`, incl. the ~6% multi-event suspect that may be human → slight under-count, client-friendly) — **no over-billing today.** The July-27 "verified customer journeys = human_likely + suspect" phrasing does NOT match the shipped code; treat `human_likely` as the billable definition. (`suspect` is 96% single-event = thin/bounce; the dashboard/attribution canonical additionally requires `event_count>1`, which is a separate, stricter filter.)

### EOS 1P durability — 1-week read + ⏳ dated re-measure trigger (Aug 5, 2026)
- **Ran the "~2–4 week" 1P durability check** from the July 30–31 identity-layer work (EOS 1P collect migration + `_shopify_y` + daily cart-token stitch cron). Read-only.
- **Mechanism confirmed working (proof-of-life):** the diagnostic "Q3" flipped **0 → 80** — anon-only canonicals now span >1 journey (was structurally impossible under 3P ITP-capped cookies). **14** span multiple days with activity since the migration. Cart-token backfill recovered **266** historical links (149 anon→known + 117 anon→anon); daily sweep adds ~0–2/day (backfill already covered the window). `_shopify_y` live: **64** `shopify_visitor:` canonicals / 19 alias edges.
- **BUT magnitude is NOT readable yet — do not claim a lift %.** Splitting the 80: only **8** are the pure durable-cookie win (same anon_id reused across journeys — the core 1P benefit); **72** are alias-merges, overwhelmingly the one-time July-31 cart-token backfill (historical recovery, won't grow). At ~6 days post-migration this is expected — the durable-cookie payoff needs anonymous visitors to RETURN (ecom return cycle 2–6 weeks). Verify-before-asserting: declaring a lift today would measure the backfill, not the fix.
- **⏳ RE-MEASURE TRIGGER: ≈ Aug 28, 2026** (4 weeks post-migration). Re-run the Q3 split; the honest lift is the GROWTH in `durable_cookie_same_id` (baseline **8**) + `spans_multiple_days` (baseline **24**) — the merged/backfill 72 is one-time and should be held constant in the comparison. Baseline snapshot for the delta: known_canon 2,233 · anon_only 59,736 · anon_multi_journey 80 (8 durable / 72 merged) · multi-day 24 · shopify_visitor 64.

### Operator-deferred — I3 (subscriber redefinition) + R3 (retention lens) SHIPPED (Aug 5, 2026)
- **Operator decided I3: subscriber = "on the email list *before* first purchase."** This unblocked R3 (which was gated on the cleaner definition). Both shipped together; DB via MCP, UI in the noted commits; each verified against live EOS before wiring.
- **I3 — de-endogenized the subscriber cohort (`i3_*` migrations + UI commit).** The old definition was "anyone in `chapter_config.email_engagement_events`" — endogenous to conversion, because post-purchase flows / checkout opt-ins put buyers on the list *after* they buy, inflating subscriber lift. New single-source-of-truth helper **`chapter_reporting.pre_purchase_subscribers(client)`**: on the list AND (never bought OR earliest email engagement predates first purchase OR first purchase predates the email-data horizon → benefit-of-the-doubt when subscription timing is unobservable). **First-purchase timestamp reads canonical_v2** (complete buyer set) so a no-session earlier purchase can't mislabel a post-purchase joiner as pre. **EOS: 6,563 → 6,453** — removed exactly the **110 observably-post-purchase joiners** (all converters by definition; verified the sync-horizon split: of 123 "subscribed-after-buying", 110 bought after the Apr-3 email horizon = observable, 13 bought before = kept ambiguous). Wired into **`matched_lift_bucket_stats`** (subscriber axis) + **`incrementality_axis_metadata`** (subscriber_count) via whitespace-tolerant regex replace; **Contribution's incremental-loss inherits it** (same engine, no extra work). Incrementality-tab axis definition copy rewritten to explain the pre-purchase framing + why it matters. Removing 110 pure-converters from the subscriber bucket lowers the endogenous inflation by construction — exactly I3's intent.
- **R3 — subscriber retention lens on the Contribution tab (`r3_*` migration + commit).** The empirical answer to what touch-level matched-incrementality structurally can't see (V5: it splits *by* subscriber status, so it can't credit email for *creating* repeat customers). New **`chapter_reporting.subscriber_retention_lens(client, now, tenure_match_days=120)`** compares pre-purchase subscribers vs non-subscriber buyers on **repeat rate + LTV** over the complete buyer set (canonical_v2) using the I3 definition. **EOS: subscribers repeat 1.7× as often (35.9% vs 21.5%) with +44% avg LTV ($157 vs $109), +50% median ($102 vs $68).** **Verify-before-building win:** this is the M1 trap (selection bias), so I checked the dominant mechanical confounder first — **tenure is balanced** (median 74d vs 64d) and **the gap survives a tenure control** (34.2% vs 21.0% within <120d-tenure buyers), so it's genuinely not just "subscribers are older." Unlike M1 (whose naive signal is *always* "synergistic" → useless), R3 is directionally right + actionable, so it ships as an **honest observational card** (Correlation-tab-style): the raw gap + a tenure-matched line + an explicit self-selection caveat ("observational, not causal; an upper bound on email's retention contribution; the counterweight to a naïve 'email is within noise' read"). Rendered after the contribution cards as empirical support for the DO-NOT-CUT verdict.
- **P1 — substrate positioning line SHIPPED on the L/I/V hero (commit after R3).** Operator ratified option 1 (the moat is the substrate, not the methods) with an accuracy correction: it's **NOT a shared "Chapter identity graph"** — each business gets its OWN isolated graph from its OWN first-party data, never pooled across clients (the correct + stronger privacy claim). Hero line reads: *"Your identity graph, not ours. Every metric here is computed per unified cross-session, cross-device customer journey — on an identity graph built from your first-party data alone, isolated to your business at the database level and never pooled across clients."* Defensible because isolation is DB-enforced (Fix #26 per-client roles + RLS) — a fact, not a marketing promise. Nuance preserved for future copy: the Recommendations flywheel shares **rule logic, never data**, so "we never pool your data across clients" stays accurate. Positioning stance locked for reuse on marketing surfaces.
- **Deferred-9 status now: 6 shipped (C3/LI3/CR2/I3/R3/P1), 3 parked.** Remaining: **AM2** (campaign-grain attribution — substantial), **9.6** (identity drill-down — bigger, thin EOS payoff), **RP4** (source-reconciliation — needs a truth-source path).

### Operator-deferred review cluster — C3 + LI3 + CR2 SHIPPED (Aug 5, 2026)
- **The 3 *unblocked* items from the review's operator-deferred set of 9.** Assessed all 9 for blockers first: **I3** (subscriber redefinition) + **P1** (positioning) need an operator decision (I3 gates **R3**); **AM2** (campaign-grain attribution), **9.6** (identity drill-down), **RP4** (source-reconciliation) are heavy/low-near-term-ROI. The 3 shipped here were pure builds reusing existing surfaces. Each verify-before-build, typechecked clean (only the known stale `.next/sctest` artifact).
- **C3 — Correlation cards → deep-links (`b… commit`).** The review's honest action for a *descriptive* tab: "form a hypothesis, then go explore it." Each Correlation card now footers two deep-links for its channel: **Cross-Source Influence anchored on the channel** (`?anchor_type=channel&anchor_channel=X`, already supported) and **Path Patterns filtered to combinations containing it**. Path Patterns had no channel filter, so added a **client-side `?channel=` filter** (its combo data is already loaded client-side — pure membership test, zero backend) with a clear chip + filter-aware empty state. No auto-prose (rejected in review — gets edge channels wrong); operator picks what to look at.
- **LI3 — ranked channel-pair discovery table on Lagged Impact.** New `chapter_reporting.lagged_impact_ranked(client, treat_start, treat_end, lag)` computes lagged return-lift for **every ordered channel pair in ONE scan** of `session_channel_entries_v1` — same math as `lagged_impact_pair` incl. LI2 right-censoring, **validated byte-identical** to the single-pair RPC on EOS `email→(direct)` (so a ranked row reconciles with the drill-in). Page now leads with the ranked table (positive-lift-first among confident pairs; within-noise/below-floor faded); clicking a row re-anchors the per-lag detail card. **EOS top confident pair: `(direct) → email` +6.26pp / +140%** (direct arrivals ~2.4× more likely to later return via email). Ranked at a representative 30d lag (falls back to the largest lag that fits the lookforward room).
- **CR2 — opt-in period-over-period on the Correlation tab.** "Compare to prior period" toggle (default off). On → each card shows its headline metric's relative delta this-period vs the immediately-preceding same-length window with a **stronger/weaker/reversed** label — but asserts a trend ONLY when **both** periods cleared the confidence gate; else says so plainly. Prior-window correlation fetched in parallel (independently cached via `priorWindow`). **Honest caveat surfaced when on:** a *population shift* between periods (traffic / identity-graph growth) isn't statistical noise the gate can catch — verified live that EOS's prior 90d straddles the shallow-history era (much smaller n, ~15% vs ~2-3% conv rates from denominator growth), so the caveat frames "stronger/weaker" as directional. Feature is correct + valuable for stable-history clients; opt-in keeps the default read uncluttered.
- **REMAINING from the deferred-9 (parked, not lost):** **I3** (operator decision: redefine "subscriber" as "subscribed *before* first purchase" → then mechanical; gates **R3** empirical email remove-and-measure), **P1** (positioning: branded composite + identity-substrate framing — a product/marketing call), **AM2** (campaign-grain attribution — substantial), **9.6** (identity drill-down — bigger, thin EOS payoff until identity coverage rises), **RP4** (source-reconciliation coverage — needs a truth-source path). Plus the OB1 data-blocked 7 (spend/product/geo/lift-history ingest).

### Dashboard review — build phase (Aug 4, 2026)
- **Follows the tab-by-tab review** (all 11 `/chapter/*` pages, logged Aug 3–4). This session is the BUILD phase: shipped the 4 HIGH bugs, one foundational data-model table, the full Lift/Incrementality/Value tab, and ~a dozen medium/quick fixes across 9 pages — then discovered the biggest remaining item was a phantom. **Every commit scoped to named Chapter files** (agency-site WIP left untouched); DB via MCP migrations (not git-tracked); each app change typechecked clean (the only tsc error is a stale `.next` `sctest/route` artifact from the retired Test-1 rig, unrelated).
- **#2 — `chapter_reporting.session_channel_entries_v1` (FOUNDATIONAL, committed `ccafdad`).** New full-population, SESSION-grain channel-entries table + `refresh_session_channel_entries_v1(client, lookback=180)` fn + dedicated cron (`/api/internal/cron/refresh-session-channel-entries`, `vercel.json` 04:45 UTC, before the 05:30 connections cron). Reuses canonical_v1's exact sessionizer + classifier over ALL resolved journeys (not just chapter-formers), resolving each session to the JOURNEY's canonical (via `last_identity_key → identity_canon`, NOT per-event identity_key — that fragmented anon ids 6× in an early iteration), filtered dashboard-canonical (`bot_class IN ('human_likely','suspect') AND event_count>1` — omitting the event_count guard flooded 228k single-event rows in iter 1). ~3 min on EOS. **Why it exists:** CSI channel connections + Lagged Impact + return-loop read journey-entry grain (one channel per journey, blind to intra-journey returns — collapsed to ~0 after the 5.1 sessionizer de-inflation) OR the chapter-scoped `chapter_session_entries_snapshot` (~15% of the population, 417 vs 2,841 for CSI's Email anchor — a naive repoint would have shrunk the anchor 7×, caught in a pre-check). This is the single correct source; repointed `lagged_impact_pair`/`_series`, `connections_panel` (3 refs), `connections_return_loop`.
- **4 HIGH bugs — all fixed:**
  - **CSI3** (channel connections empty post-5.1) — repointed `connections_panel` channel branch to `session_channel_entries_v1`; Email downstream now populates (1,040 direct returns, was `[]`).

Stance locked: bots are ALREADY excluded from metrics + billed verified-journeys, and Chapter can't observe upstream-blocked bots. Verified live: EOS finding now reads "72% bot-like — already excluded from your metrics and billing", severity low.

Now shows both denominators (`<ids> identities / <chapters> chapters`); verified the gate field mapping in `buildIncGates` (ids_with vs n_with vs days_n_with) against the review's live-RPC finding.

- **Customer Journeys CJ1-CJ4** (`55e715d` + 2 migrations): **CJ1** (migration `cj1_identity_search_match_order_number`) — `identity_search` falls back to the customer-facing Shopify `order_number`/`name` (`103877` / `#103877`, `#`-normalized) when the internal `order_id` misses; verified all three inputs resolve the same person. **CJ2** — the real bug: page.tsx fell back to `listRows[0]` and the detail panel gated on `selectedRow` (list membership), so a searched customer beyond the top-50-by-LTV silently rendered the WRONG (top-of-list) person or the empty state. Now an explicit `?identity` is always honored + the panel derives its header facts (converted/LTV/last-purchase) from the fetched detail, with a "searched customer, not in the list" note. **CJ3** (migration `cj3_journeys_stats_converted_scoped_ltv`) — the health bar showed Avg LTV $1.32 / Median $0.00 for EOS (56,747 identities, 0.8% buyers); `journeys_overview_stats` gained `avg_ltv_converted`/`median_ltv_converted` and the bar now reads "Avg per buyer" $166 / "Median per buyer" $99. **CJ4** — the list header reflects the active action filter + explains (tip) the list is top-50-by-value of the filtered cohort (so filtering can look similar; the ×N badge is where the filter shows).
- **AM1** (`67bfe1b`): Attribution Models "Channels: All / Paid" scope toggle (mirrors CSI scoping). Narrows the VIEW only — each channel's % stays its share of the ALL-channel attributed total (not re-normalized), so the single-model card keeps totals + unattributed math over the full set and just filters which bars render; compare bump/table use the scoped subset. Empty-scope note for clients with no paid (EOS). Campaign-grain attribution (**AM2**) stays a separate future build.
- **LI2** (`fffe87c` + migration `li2_lagged_impact_right_censor`): `lagged_impact_pair` now right-censors — caps the effective treatment end at `data_horizon − lag_days` so a touch measured within lag_days of the horizon (outcome window running into the unobserved future) isn't miscounted as "did not return". Both cohorts only count touches whose full lag window has elapsed; empties honestly if that pushes the end before the start. Verified EOS (email→direct lag 30: treated 417/baseline 223, gate ok). `_series` is a per-bucket volume backdrop (no outcome window) → unchanged. Hero copy notes recent touches are excluded + larger lags analyze a slightly earlier slice.
- **Recommendations "rebuild" REFUTED (4th verify-before-building win of the review).** The review's REC4 ("14 of 18 rules are shells → implement them") + the todo "Recommendations rebuild" were WRONG. Reality: **18 of 20 rule files have real evaluators + are enabled + firing** (only R1.4 platform-discrepancy + R5.1 ROAS-vs-LTV are the intentional capability-gated `enabled=false` stubs). The Aug 3 cron (post July-6-to-Aug-3 hang self-heal) ran clean for every client — `rules_evaluated: 18, status: ok` (EOS fired 2, NSC 3, adsforgood 1). The review saw an EMPTY Recommendations page (because the cron was hung) and inferred the rules didn't exist. R6.3 "Outlier behavior detected" is already the anomaly/surprise scanner. So **no rebuild** — only the R1 *GChat flywheel* (operator promotes a surprise to a named rule that runs for all tenants) and OB1 (fold Observations questions into rules — likely redundant with the 18) remain as genuinely-optional future work.
- **Method note (reinforced):** verify every number/hypothesis against the running system before building — the review's *data* findings held (I1 mislabel, I2 saturation, V5 over-control, CJ2/CJ3 all confirmed via live RPC), but its *build-scope* hypotheses were repeatedly overstated (CSI3 population-shrink pre-check, and now the Recommendations "shells"). Base-rate awareness was the review's through-line and is now on the L/I/V tab.
- **CSI6 — RESOLVED (Aug 5) as verified-marginal (5th verify-before-building win), no net DB change.** The 9.3 page/campaign lift base was a fixed rolling 90d while the anchor numerator is the selected window (default 30d) — hypothesized to deflate page/campaign lift ~3×. Built the 30d-match fix (rebuilt `connections_page_base_v1` MV to 30d + `campaign_base` 90d→30d via a zero-transcription `pg_get_functiondef`+`replace` DO-block), then **verified against live data before asserting**: `base_rate` is a normalized SHARE (page-viewers ÷ all-active), so it's largely window-INVARIANT — EOS homepage base moved only 35.2% (90d) → 32.2% (30d), ~9%, so homepage lift ~0.44→~0.48 (NOT →1×); the homepage's sub-1 lift is genuine (direct visitors rarely re-visit the homepage downstream). And a 30d base is materially noisier for low-volume clients (projectagram near-empty in 30d). So 90d is the more stable, robust baseline. **Reverted both DB objects to 90d** (`__ALL__` back to 16,997; net no DB change; the connections snapshot was never rebuilt during the excursion so it stays consistent, self-heals nightly). Kept only an honest footer note (`b4e62dd`) that the page/campaign lift baseline is a stable 90-day long-run popularity rate (channel lift is unaffected — its base is window-scoped). MCP gotcha reconfirmed: the `CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW … AS` full page_view scan client-timed-out at ~90-120s but completed server-side (~200s+); poll `pg_stat_activity` catalog-only — reading the MV itself queues behind the DDL's lock.
- **R1 GChat flywheel — SHIPPED (Aug 5, `85f7671`).** The weekly Recommendations cron now collects `emerging_patterns`-theme findings (R6.1 new-channel-material, R6.2 sustained-trend, R6.3 >3σ daily journey/order/revenue outlier) that are **new or newly escalated** this run and posts one digest to the **Chapter Data Alerts** space (`postToGChat` → `CHAPTER_GCHAT_WEBHOOK_URL`, the same webhook as the operational/data alerts, per operator ask) so an operator can review a surprise and, if it's worth watching everywhere, promote the pattern to a named rule that runs for every tenant. Silent when nothing is new. Collected in `runForClient` (theme + new/changed state filter), aggregated + posted in `GET` after the failure-alert block. Verified the theme filter against live data (real historical R6.3 outliers exist: NSC journeys 40 vs 0-20, projectagram revenue $222 vs $0-39 — all currently `resolved`, so the flywheel is correctly quiet until the next new outlier). Gated `?test_flywheel=1` affordance posts a labeled SAMPLE digest for one-shot routing/format verification without running the engine. R6.3 IS the surprise-scanner detection the review's "R1" referred to; this adds the alert loop on top. **OB1** (fold Observations questions into rules — likely mostly redundant with the 18 live rules; needs a coverage-gap check first) stays optional.
- **OB1 — Observations engine coverage-check + partial fold + RETIRE (Aug 5).** The Observations engine was still firing nightly (05:00 UTC) into a tab retired from nav (`8dc900b`) — producing findings nobody saw. Coverage-checked its 16 runner-backed questions (of 27 catalogued) against the 18 live Recommendations rules: **most are redundant** (I1↔R1.3 stitching, I2↔R1.1 bot, A1↔R2.2 under-credit, A3↔R6.1/6.2, M4↔R6.1/R3.3, R4↔R5.3; I4 pipeline-freshness is already covered by the monitoring crons). **Folded the genuine gaps into 4 new named rules** (commit `fe80c3a` + MCP: 4 `chapter_reporting.rec_*` metrics RPCs + 4 `chapter_recommendations.rules` rows, all `enabled`): **R1.5** Direct first-touch creep (from Obs I3 — EOS 61% direct), **R2.5** Single-touch new-customer acquisition (from Obs A4 — organic search 60%, the "decisive acquisition" signal), **R4.5** Repeat-purchase tempo shift (new; Obs R2 had no runner — EOS repeat gap 23→27d, +17.6% lengthening), **R5.4** Lapsed cohort opportunity (new; Obs R3 had no runner — 210 EOS buyers quiet 3–12mo, $22K prior value). Each is a thin evaluator over a metrics RPC, tested against live EOS data before wiring (verify-before-asserting). Next Monday's cron evaluates 22 rules. **C2 — SHIPPED (Aug 5, `a65043c`) as R4.4, after a redesign.** Building it revealed the original page_view → add_to_cart step is bot-polluted (page_view distinct-counts include bots that never add to cart — EOS flagged a spurious 1.6% "worst drop") AND is the sole 116s cost. Dropped it → C2 is now a **cart-abandonment funnel** (add_to_cart → view_cart → purchase — all human-ish, low-volume), runs LIVE in 901ms (no snapshot/cron; the `funnel_step_snapshot` table + refresh fn built during the attempt were dropped). EOS: view_cart → purchase fell 43.1% → 38.9% — a real checkout leak. **R5 — SHIPPED (Aug 5, `b3e2171`) as a value-momentum PAIR** (operator asked for both windowed + cumulative). The insight: don't run windowed-vs-cumulative separately (two weak halves) — CROSS them (cumulative LTV tier = stature × trailing-90d-vs-prior-90d value = momentum) and split by direction. **R5.5** Top-value customers cooling off (top-tercile-by-LTV whose recent spend fell >50% — EOS 83 customers, $22.5K LTV, 66 fully silent; the urgent churn signal, distinct from R5.4's broad win-back pool). **R5.6** Customers accelerating (existing buyers up 50%+ recently — EOS 59, $14.6K; expansion/nurture, low severity). One shared RPC `rec_r5_value_momentum` (v2 buyer set), two thin evaluators, verified live. **Engine now at 25 rules.** **M1 — INVESTIGATED, deferred as refuted-for-now (Aug 5, verify-before-building win #Nth).** Channel cannibalization = "two channels co-occurring adds no incremental value." Confirmed `matched_lift_bucket_stats` can't express it (its `bucket` is a demographic cohort — subscriber/location/value_band — not channel-B-presence), so it'd need a bespoke pairwise conditional-lift RPC. Prototyped the naive version on EOS (90d): cell sizes are ample (email×direct = 1,844 both / 8,115 direct-only / 2,231 email-only) — NOT sample-blocked. **But the naive conditional-conversion comparison is fatally confounded:** `{email+direct}` converts 15.5% vs `direct-only` 2.6% and `email-only` 2.2% — which naively reads as huge synergy, but that's multi-touch SELECTION BIAS (two-channel identities are self-selected more-engaged), not causal lift (same reason V5 flagged email's adjusted lift as "within noise"). So a simple cannibalization rule would ALWAYS report "synergistic," never catch real cannibalization → misleading; won't ship it. The trustworthy version needs engagement-adjusted (Lin's-style, control for touches/path length) pairwise conditional lift — research-grade, and fragile at 1-client scale. **Verdict: keep M1 deferred** (quick version misleading, correct version disproportionate). OB1 delivered **7 rules** (R1.5/R2.5/R4.4/R4.5/R5.4/R5.5/R5.6); M1 is the one honest non-build. **Data-blocked backlog — 7 questions that can't run until Chapter ingests the enabling data, preserve as "future rules pending data":** A5/A6/S4 (ad-spend connector), S3/C1 (product-catalog ingest), S2 (geo spread), M2 (lift-test history). **Engine retired:** removed the `run-observations` cron from `vercel.json` (route file left dead-but-untriggered; `chapter_observations` schema + data can be dropped in a future cleanup). MCP gotcha reconfirmed: `information_schema.columns` excludes materialized views — use `pg_attribute` for MV columns.
- **v1-vs-v2 buyer-population correction (Aug 5, `7a53c02` + MCP).** Verifying the new R5.4 card surfaced a suspiciously-round "of **1000**" denominator: `chapter_channel_paths_canonical_v1` is the SESSION-ATTRIBUTED subset (1000 EOS buyers), while `canonical_v2` = v1 + no-session fallback = the COMPLETE buyer set (1120). Not a cap (no LIMIT in the v1 loader) — v1 is *by design* the session subset. **Principle locked:** buyer-population / LTV / recency questions must read **v2** (complete); channel-attribution questions read **v1** (v2's `(direct)` fallback would pollute them). Fixed: **R4.5** repeat-tempo + **R5.4** lapsed-cohort RPCs repointed v1→v2 (R5.4 210/1000/$22.4K → **252/1120/$26.2K**; R1.5 + R2.5 correctly stay on v1). And the **Customer Journeys** RPCs (`journeys_overview_stats`, `journeys_overview_list`, `journey_detail_chapters`) — which had the same latent undercount, disagreeing with the v2-based headline revenue — repointed v1→v2 via a guarded DO-block replace (each had exactly one v1-view ref, no `_v1_snapshot` ref). EOS 30d: Converted 451→**475**, Total LTV $74.9K→**$82.3K**, Avg-per-buyer $166→**$173**. "Converted" now means "made a purchase" (not "…we could session-attribute") and reconciles with Raw Performance. DB-only (RPC swaps); no TS/UI change.
- **REMAINING (documented, not lost):** M1 cannibalization is investigated + deferred-as-refuted (naive version confounded by engagement selection bias; the trustworthy engagement-adjusted-pairwise-conditional-lift version is a research-grade build parked indefinitely — see OB1 entry). Plus the operator-deferred set **C3** (Correlation→Path Patterns/CSI deep-links), **I3** (subscriber cohort-def decision), **AM2** (campaign-grain attribution), **LI3** (ranked top channel pairs by lagged impact), **R3** (empirical email remove-and-measure test), **P1** (positioning: branded composite + identity-substrate framing), **9.6** (identity drill-down), **CR2** (two-period side-by-side card view), **RP4** (source-reconciliation coverage build). A UI footnote on the exact effective-window shortening for LI2 is a nice-to-have.

### Agency-site SEO — three interactive calculators + EDDM/direct-mail ship + sitemap/canonicals; welcome step-0 fix; EOS phantom-journey diagnosis (Aug 2–4, 2026)
- **Marketing-site SEO work (agency pages, not the Chapter dashboard). Interactive-calculator infra + two live ships + one date-gated. All spec-driven (`quality_first`). Standing directive: title tags `| ads for Good` lowercase, plans-hub the lone no-suffix exception.**
- **Reusable interactive-calculator pattern (established this batch).** For markdown education guides: a `[[SENTINEL]]` paragraph in the post body that [PostBody.tsx](src/app/for-people/education/PostBody.tsx)'s `p` renderer swaps for a client component — the H2, intro copy, and FAQ stay in the (server-rendered) markdown for SEO; only the widget hydrates. For `.tsx` service pages: the component is imported + placed directly in JSX. Calculators are **disabled-safe** — where a config value can go stale (EDDM's USPS rate), the component renders a "being updated" notice rather than a total from a guessed/null value; the two reader-input-only calculators have no config at all.
- **EDDM cost calculator — LIVE 8/04** (commit `d37de29`). Per `eddm-calculator-amendment-spec.md`. Added an interactive calculator to `/for-people/education/what-is-eddm` ([EddmCalculator.tsx](src/app/for-people/education/EddmCalculator.tsx)): 5 fields (households + print + drops/design/response-rate), live math, USPS postage **$0.26/piece as of 4 August 2026** + **5,000/ZIP daily cap** in a single `EDDM_CONFIG` block (the only specific postage figure on the site; prose stays hedged). Retitled *"…Costs and Rules"*, price-led meta, amended the cost FAQ, added *"Is EDDM cost effective?"*. Moved the guide's `publishAt` to 8/04 and shipped it **together with the retargeted `/for-businesses/direct-mail` service page** so their cross-link went live simultaneously. Worked examples verified byte-exact.
- **Digital Rent calculator — LIVE now** (commit `e94384e`) on `/for-businesses/reduce-software-costs` ([DigitalRentCalculator.tsx](src/app/for-businesses/reduce-software-costs/DigitalRentCalculator.tsx)). Per `digital-rent-calculator-amendment-spec.md`. Repeatable subscription rows (name/cost/Monthly-Yearly, ≤20), 3/5/10-year horizons (5-yr emphasized), per-tool 5-yr split ("Everything else" groups unnamed), optional compounding annual-increase, the mandated **candour line verbatim**. No config, no build price/payback/ROI, no email/export/persistence/CTA. Placed as a full-width block after the hero (the "Over your business lifetime" H2 lives in a hero side-card), intro "Here's the actual number." + one FAQ sentence changed. Examples reproduce exactly.
- **Ad Budget Floor Checker — publishes 8/05 with its guide** (commit `e94384e`) on `ppc-management-pricing` ([AdBudgetFloorChecker.tsx](src/app/for-people/education/AdBudgetFloorChecker.tsx)). Per `ad-budget-floor-checker-spec.md`. Ad-spend + quoted-fee → overhead % + **one hedged band verdict** selected on ad spend (`<$1k` below · `$1k–2,999` grey · `≥$3k` above, boundary inclusive at the low end), plus self-managed (fee=0) + fee-exceeds-spend states. Buyer-side labels, no verdict headline/score/colour/CTA/rate — the % is the payload. **Operator-directed spec override:** added a second internal link — a *"one exception worth naming"* caveat after the "$500 a month" line, linking to `/for-businesses`, because the guide's $1k floor otherwise misrepresents afG's model (flat fee **on top** of ad spend + multiple projects for one management fee, not an ad-management-only cut). Guide `publishAt` moved 8/08 → **8/05**. `digital-ads` (the only page linking to this guide, no other gated dep) queued to push **8/05** so the cross-link resolves same-day.
- **XML sitemap rewrite** (commit `5520a6d`) — was a hardcoded 17-URL list missing ~10 live pages + every blog post; now lists all live `/for-businesses` + core + for-people pages and pulls education posts from the registry filtered by `isLive` (date-gated guides enter on their `publishAt` via `revalidate=3600`, never pointing at a 404). **Self-referential canonicals** (commit `ec0c634`) added across all live marketing pages via `alternates.canonical` (+ education `[slug]` via `generateMetadata`); the 12 held service pages' canonicals ride the 8/08 batch. Both agenda items from memory `project_sitemap_canonicals_gsc`.
- **Welcome-sequence step-0 backfill** (commit `190fe2e`, self-serve SaaS). The instant "Welcome — your first 2 minutes" email (step 0) was sent **only** from the auth-callback fresh-provision branch, so reactivation / reused-email signups never got it and the daily cron couldn't backfill (`evaluateWelcome` started at step 1). Added `consider(0, true, true)` so the cron sends step 0 whenever it wasn't already recorded, ahead of later steps — no-ops on the happy path, still gated by `subscribed`/`deletionRequested`. Surfaced when a k_man reactivation test never received step 0.
- **EOS phantom-journey diagnosis (READ-ONLY; the fix is being handled in a parallel Chapter session).** Investigating an "unusual live visitors" report: EOS logged **~12k "journeys" today, ~80% of them phantom** — journey rows with an identity + `opt_out` consent but **zero `pixel_events`**. Root cause: **`/api/consent` upserts a `chapter_journey.journeys` row on every JS-rendering bot page-load** (the pixel's consent-init fires), and because the bot is `opt_out` the consent gate blocks all event logging → a journey with no events. Today: 13,686 consent_events / 12,419 distinct journeys, **100% opt_out**. Bots: **meta-externalads spiked 0 → ~6k on 8/03** (Meta crawler — no ad campaign needed; link-preview / catalog re-scrape), **Googlebot chronic ~100–2,800/day** (rising with the SEO-page crawl). **Not a Shopify miscategorization** — Blockify (~690 real visitors) + Shopify (352 visits) correctly exclude these; **Chapter over-counts** by minting journeys for bot consent-inits. Inflates raw/billable journey count + seeds ~9k/day bot identities; nightly bot-classifier still flags 0/1-event bot journeys so dashboards/attribution are ~unaffected. **Fix leads (for the Chapter window):** bot-UA filter at the ingest/consent/identify layer, and/or don't mint a journey on an opt-out consent-only init before a real page_view.

### Cross-Source Influence 9.3 SHIPPED + Lift/Incrementality/Value tab audit (Aug 3–4, 2026)
- **9.3 (dimension-agnostic "All" connections panel) — SHIPPED, commit `355ac68`.** Completes the 9.3 build (Stages 1–4). Cross-Source Influence now defaults to an **"All" view** that unions channel + page + campaign connections into one lift-ranked, dimension-tagged panel; explicit Channels/Pages/Campaigns toggles narrow to one dimension.
  - **DB (`connections_panel` RPC, via MCP migrations, not git-tracked):** new **campaign branch** (email `chapter_config.email_engagement_events` clicks up/downstream of the anchor; live base over the client's ~40 campaigns → comparable lift + median-touch); **page branch gained real lift** via the `connections_page_base_v1` MV (page n_identities / __ALL__ base_pop). `p_connection_type` now accepts `'channel'|'page'|'campaign'`. Two ambiguity bugs fixed during build: `n_identities` OUT-param collided with the MV column in ORDER BY (→ ordinal `4`) and in the `page_base_pop`/`campaign_base_pop` CTEs (→ table-aliased). Channel branch byte-identical to Stage 1.
  - **App:** `page.tsx` fetches all 3 dims per direction in parallel (each snapshot-backed) + concats — rows pre-tagged via `connected_thing_type`. `InfluenceClient` renders per-row dimension badges (page/email), campaign-row re-anchoring, a 4-way All/Channels/Pages/Campaigns toggle for every anchor type, neutral "Connection" header + "seen" subtitles. `refresh-connections-snapshots` cron enumerates the campaign dimension.
  - **Validated on EOS:** `/fabrics` lift 1.98 downstream of direct, homepage 0.44 (base 35.2%), a campaign at 0.13. **Self-heals fully on the next 05:30 UTC cron** (existing page/campaign snapshot rows were built by the pre-lift RPC; until tonight, page lift shows "—" and campaign falls to the fast live RPC — cosmetic, resolves nightly).
- **Lift / Incrementality / Value tab audit (review only — NO builds shipped; findings logged + queued as todos).** Reviewed the 3 L/I/V tabs on EOS against the rubric: *does it give the owner an ACTION (not data-for-data's-sake), then accuracy → makes-sense → statistical rigor.* Verified every number against the live RPCs before asserting. **The through-line: all 3 tabs share one missing primitive — base-rate / saturation awareness — and one missing habit — routing the "so what" to the Recommendations engine.**
  - **Correlation (concept strong, honest; action + sense are the gaps):**
    - **C1** — `pickHeadline()` ([LiftClient.tsx:142](src/app/chapter/(authed)/lift/LiftClient.tsx#L142)) picks largest |Δ%|, so path-length-proxy metrics (time-to-close +619%, touches +575% — the *same fact twice*) grab the headline on the Direct card, burying the real story (conv −52.8%, AOV +24.8%). Incrementality already excludes time-to-close from headline candidacy ([LiftClient.tsx:569](src/app/chapter/(authed)/lift/LiftClient.tsx#L569)); Correlation never got it. **Fix = deviation-based, redundancy-aware headline** (don't headline a metric fully explained by its correlated partner) — deliberately KEEPS the good case (a new channel's −50% time-to-close is a *deviation* → headlines). Blanket-excluding time-to-close was rejected (operator: a fast-closing new channel is exactly the signal you want). ~30–40 LOC. **Approved.**
    - **C2** — 4 metrics read as co-equal. **Fix = group Value (conv, AOV) vs Character (days, touches).**
    - **C3** — card states facts, no verb. **Fix = deep-link each card → Path Patterns / Cross-Source Influence filtered to that channel's converting chapters** (the honest action for a descriptive tab = "form a hypothesis → go explore/test it"). Reuses built surfaces; no auto-prose (rejected — gets edge channels wrong).
    - Drill-down that *found the value*: EOS converting chapters WITHOUT Direct (90d) = single-touch same-day closes on organic search + email; **email single-touch closes same-day at $88 AOV — above Direct's $90.98 at 1/10th the touches.** Email = decisive high-value acquisition, not just nurture. This is a **Recommendations rule**, not a card feature.
  - **Incrementality (rigor more mature — gates work; gaps = mislabeled n + saturated cohort):**
    - **I1 (real bug)** — the bucket header "n" (e.g. Subscribers "n=217 with") is the **AOV chapter sample** (`n_with`), but conversion rate divides by **identities** (`ids_with`=61). Off by 3.5×; an owner reads "90.2% of 217." **Fix = show both denominators or label n per-metric.**
    - **I2** — saturated cohorts display meaningless conversion "lift": EOS Subscribers convert at **96.7% at baseline** (n=61) — no headroom. Tool correctly gates it "within noise" + headlines AOV, but the panel still invites "subscribers convert at 90%!" misread. **Fix = saturation guard** (near-ceiling base or tiny-n → demote/annotate that metric).
    - **I3 (methodology decision, bigger)** — "subscriber" ≈ known-email ≈ captured-at-checkout → cohort endogenous to conversion. Cleaner def = "subscribed *before* first purchase."
    - Headline *works well here*: "lift concentrated in Non-subscribers (+135.4% conversion)" is the real action — **organic search is EOS's new-customer acquisition engine; subscribers would convert anyway.** (Also a Recommendations rule.)
  - **Contribution / Value (the hero tab — DO-NOT-CUT verdicts are the most decision-shaped thing in the feature):**
    - **V1** — matrix labels clipped at the right edge (Referral, Organic Social). **Fix = pad x-domain / flip label left near edge.**
    - **V2** — "Contribution Index /100" reads as a % (operator + any owner misread it). It's a **relative 0–100 composite** (participation + linear-revenue share + recurrence, normalized across channels). **Fix = reframe label** ("Footprint score · 100 = most-embedded channel").
    - **V3** — 4 quadrants unexplained. **Fix = hover explainer per quadrant + its action.**
    - **V4** — Direct gets "if it disappeared / DO NOT CUT" — an **impossible action** (can't turn off return visitors). Direct is a floor, not a lever. **Fix = exclude from Contribution OR reframe as "return-visitor floor, not a spend lever."**
    - **V5 (the most important finding)** — matched incrementality **under-measures retention/CRM channels.** Email's incrementality is computed on the **subscriber axis**, which conditions on email's own outcome (email's job is to *create* subscribers + repeat habit; splitting by subscriber status regresses that out — over-controlling / collider bias). So "within noise / CI straddles 0" is honest about *"marginal effect of one more email touch on an already-saturated subscriber"* but is a **known underestimate** of email's business value. The tab's footprint side ($25,193 linear, index 49, "connective tissue — DO NOT CUT") captures the real value and the verdict is *correct* — the tab is the antidote to cutting email off a naive incrementality read. **Fix = retention-channel caveat** on the incremental-loss band. **R3 (queued): empirically test the "remove email and it tanks" hunch the honest way — subscriber vs matched non-subscriber repeat-rate + LTV** (measures the upstream value the touch-level design misses; don't bank the hunch, measure it).
  - **Recommendations / cross-cutting (the "so what" layer):**
    - **R1** — **surprise-scanner meta-rule**: a generic anomaly rule (flags any channel that's an outlier vs peers / vs what its correlated metrics predict) that *guarantees the Recommendations front door is never empty* even when no named rule covers a finding. Alerts operator via **GChat → operator promotes good ones to named rules that then run for ALL tenants** (the flywheel: one client's surprise → product improvement). Same base-rate/deviation primitive as C1/I2/V5.
    - **R2** — new named rules mined from this review: decisive-acquisition (email fast/high-AOV), SEO-acquires-non-subscribers, retention-underestimate (email CRM blind spot), saturated-cohort suppressor.
  - **P1 (positioning decision):** do NOT claim Chapter invented these metrics — matched-cohort incrementality (Meta Lift/Haus/Measured/Northbeam), participation/footprint (Google assisted conversions, Markov/Shapley), the incremental×footprint 2×2 (BCG matrix) are all recognizable; an overclaim is a credibility attack. **Claim the defensible thing: the substrate** (these computed per unified cross-session/device customer journey on Chapter's first-party identity graph + chapter model — the moat), a **branded composite** ("Chapter Contribution Index™", NPS-style), and the **honest, noise-gated, retention-aware POV** + the recommendations flywheel.
  - **The one build that unlocks four fixes:** a **base-rate / expected-value module** — Correlation C1 (headline), Incrementality I2 (saturation guard), Contribution V5 (retention caveat), Recommendations R1 (surprise-scanner). Build once, four surfaces.
  - **Review method note (reinforces prior lesson):** verify every number against the running RPC before asserting; the data findings held (I1 mislabel, I2 saturation, V5 over-control all confirmed via `incrementality_channel_overview` + `chapter_purchase_summary` queries on EOS). Operator drives via screenshots + questions; I trace the RPC behind what's on screen. **Remaining L/I/V review debt: every surface of these tabs deserves a fuller pass later; this audit covered the default-view cards + matrix.**

All patterns regex-validated against real hosts (no collisions).

The sessionizer loads global+per-client non-revoked patterns and **drops matching session entries** from paths (guarded `cardinality=0` so empty list keeps everything). Verified: admin.shopify dropped, seamwork/google/null kept. Ingest helper (`tracking-ignore.ts`) unaffected (it queries by specific type). Reusable — muting the next junk referrer is a one-row INSERT.

  - **6.5(b)+(c)** (`fed9bdb`) — Attribution Models per-model blind-spot slot. First-touch states its blind spot; 6.5(a) coverage readout awaits 6.4. Low-sample floor (20 chapters).

Gated rows faded + labelled; footer discloses the floor + that the tab does NOT correct for multiple comparisons. Fully client-side (fields already in the snapshot).
