Refactor threaded `page_url` through the CTEs + materialized `_cse` once → feeds both the byte-identical v1 aggregate and the new table. **Validated:** v1 before/after `recon_mismatches=0` (session table STRING_AGGs exactly to v1's channel_path across all 1,475 EOS chapters); v2 baseline intact; the 13 before/after `channel_path` diffs are all `email_sha256` canonicals with membership-change/relabel signatures = ~1h `identity_canon` drift, not a bug (5.1's own regression saw the same ~1%). - **Group A of the tab-by-tab dashboard review (`~/Downloads/chapter_dashboard_review_running.md`) closed out — 6 of 6 items shipped.** All are `/chapter/*` operator-surface work; each committed as its own focused commit on `main`.
  - **5.3** (`2aed412`) — Path Patterns distinguishes "no revenue" from "revenue not captured": `path_combinations_overview` gained `chapters_with_revenue`; AOV now divides by revenue-bearing chapters (`sum(revenue)/NULLIF(count(*) FILTER (WHERE revenue>0),0)`); UI renders "—" not `$0.00` + a partial-coverage "N/M" badge. EOS no-op verified (revenue total unchanged).
  - **9.1** (`15f3e15`) — Cross-Source Influence base-rate + lift-over-expected columns. `connections_panel` RETURNS TABLE gained `base_rate`/`lift` (channel branch computes base_rate over `valid_journey`, lift = pct_of_anchor/base_rate, ORDER BY lift DESC). **Page-anchor branch returns NULL** for both — full page_view-universe scan exceeds the 60s RPC cap; deferred to a per-page identity-count MV. UI: Lift column + color-coded value + "base X%" sub-line + base-rate caption.
  - **1.1 + 1.3** (`8dc900b`) — Observations retired from the operator nav (`isStaff` gate in Sidebar + route redirect to `/chapter/recommendations` for non-staff); engine keeps running. "Suggest what Chapter should watch for" drawer moved off Observations → Recommendations.
  - **9.4** (`c163a6c`) — Cross-Source Influence Cohort removed as an anchor (backend resolution branch preserved; re-add as a *filter* later once the panel RPC accepts filter args).
  - **8.2** (`4201923`) — Customer Journeys identity search entry point. New client-scoped `chapter_reporting.identity_search(client, identity_key?, order_id?)` RPC (SECURITY DEFINER) resolves email / order id / phone / platform customer id / canonical key → canonical + coverage counts. Server action `journeys/_actions.ts` hashes via the SHARED `hash.ts` (provably round-trips to what capture stored), enforces `canAccessClient` (never trusts the passed key), rate-limits 30/5min per viewer, and audits every attempt via `logPiiView` (never the plaintext term). A hit navigates to `?identity=<canonical>` so the existing detail panel (chapters, events, merged aliases with `method` + deterministic dot) + PII-view audit render. Three outcomes distinguished (no identity / found-no-sessions / found); exact-match only, stated in the empty state.
- **Two attribution-infra fixes (DB-only; applied to prod via MCP, not git-tracked):**
  - **Snapshot-cron channel vocab mismatch** (`3deb03d`, code) — the connections-snapshot cron enumerated channels as `direct`/`organic_search`/`paid_search`/`paid_social`, but the stored vocab (canonical_v1/v2 `channel_path`) + the UI's `CHANNEL_OPTIONS` use `(direct)`/`organic search`/`paid search`/`organic social`. So the snapshot key never matched the anchor_key the app looks up — the highest-traffic anchors (`(direct)`=346, `organic search`=108) silently missed the snapshot and fell to the slow live-RPC path every load (undoing Sprint 1A's acceleration), while the cron wrote dead rows nothing read. **Perf bug, not correctness** (fallback returns right data). Fix: `CHANNELS` now mirrors the stored/queryable vocab exactly (also dropped never-present meta/tiktok/affiliate/sms). Dead old-vocab rows purged; corrected-vocab rows repopulate on the 05:30 UTC cron.
  - **Referral substring bugs + webmail hardening** (migration `fix_referral_substring_and_webmail_classifier`) — in `refresh_canonical_v1_snapshot`'s session-entry classifier: `%t.co%` was matching **13,823** chatgp**t.co**m-type referrers and `%x.com%` was matching **806** gm**x.com**/netflix.com referrers, both mislabeled *organic social* → replaced with host-boundary regex `~* '://([^/]+\.)?t\.co(/|$)'` (validated: matches real t.co/x.com incl. subdomains, rejects the collisions). Also added webmail referrers (`mail.google.com`, Gmail app `com.google.android.gm`, Yahoo/Outlook mail) → **email**, placed *before* the `%google%` organic-search branch (**34,499** webmail events were at risk of organic-search misclassification; the email-UTM'd subset was already correct, hence "dormant" at EOS). Only channel *labels* change — chapter boundaries/counts/revenue untouched, so 5.1's invariant holds. Only one function carries the classifier (no half-fix). **Self-heals forward** on the nightly 03:30 UTC incremental chain; historical dormant canonicals keep old labels until an optional one-time full v1 rebuild (deferred — worth scheduling given the t.co volume).
- **Deferred (logged, not requested now):** 1.2 (Observations→Recommendations evidence infra — rules-schema col + evaluator helper); 9.4 cohort-as-filter (needs panel-RPC filter args); 9.1 page base-rate per-page identity-count MV; `admin.shopify.com` → ignore list (needs referrer-support added to `tracking_ignore_list`, which only supports `ua_substring`/`email_sha256` today); optional full v1 rebuild to apply the classifier fix retroactively. **Bigger review items still open:** Lift/Incrementality/Value (never reviewed, now unblocked by 7.7); Customer Journeys UI review; Recommendations rule verification; Lifecycle Overview keep-or-delete; Group C re-measurement of anon→known lift in ~2–4 weeks.

  - **5 spoke posts shipped** under Marketing Playbook, each with distinct preview image + FAQ/JSON-LD + up-link to its pillar:
    - Popup cluster (→ smart-prompts): `privy-alternatives` (roundup), `email-popup-examples` (trigger-grouped, 4 real screenshots from the operator), `klaviyo-popups` (how-to).
    - Link cluster (→ smart-links): `link-management-tools` (roundup — absorbs "rebrandly alternative" + "clickmagick alternative" long-tail), `first-party-click-tracking` (informational differentiator — ITP/cookie decay).
  - Both pillars got a **"Related reading"** block linking down to their spokes; cross-link anchors fixed for consistency (smart-prompts→smart-links now "link management software").
- **Deferred (deliberate):** popup Page 3 was built at operator's override, but the link cluster's **Spoke 3 (`rebrandly-clickmagick-alternatives`) NOT built** — the spec gates it behind a 90-day check and Spoke 1 already absorbs those terms, so building it now would cannibalize. Also flagged but not built: `/for-businesses/education` clone (rejected for one-blog), mobile + "what-not-to-do" screenshots for the email-examples page (operator to supply), a real comparison *table* on the roundup posts (would need a `remark-gfm` dependency; rendered as honest structured lists instead).
- **Guardrails honored on all spoke copy:** no invented stats/customer numbers, no competitor pricing without a date-check, no verbatim competitor marketing copy, no imitating incumbents' proof-heavy structure (won on specificity). **Nav:** added "Education" under the About dropdown (desktop + mobile) → `/for-people/education`.

### Dashboard review 5.1 + 7.7 — canonical sessionizer de-inflation + snapshot reconciliation (July 31, 2026)
- **Keystone build from the tab-by-tab review** (the two highest-leverage items, done as one cutover since 5.1 rebuilds the exact snapshot 7.7 reconciles). Pure-DB change; zero app code.
- **5.1 — per-event session split.** `chapter_attribution.refresh_canonical_v1_snapshot`'s `flagged` CTE opened a new session on ANY event carrying an external referrer. `document.referrer` persists across every event on a page, so one arrival firing page_view + scroll + hover + time_on_page minted 4–5 "sessions." EOS mean path was **26.1**, worst chapter a **1,848-segment** `organic search → organic search → …` (one customer, one arrival).
  - **Data-driven formulation (rejected the doc's suggested (a)+(c) for (a)+(b)).** 21d EOS diagnostic: **~87% of external-referrer events are engagement events** (visibility_change/hover_intent/time_on_page/page_exit/scroll_depth), each ~98% carrying the SAME referrer as the prior event. And of external-ref page_views, **49.6% repeat the prior referrer** (SPA re-fire — `ChapterLoader` re-fires page_view on route change with the arrival referrer intact). The 30s floor (c) is time-fragile (only 38.9% of page_views are <30s from prior). So the fix = **(a)** referrer break only on navigation events (`page_view`,`redirect_click`) + **(b)** only when referrer actually *changed* from the prior event (`referrer IS DISTINCT FROM prev_ref`, new `LAG(referrer)` in `ordered`). (b) is single-pass and directly kills both the engagement-event and SPA-repeat mechanisms; the only cost — same-source re-arrivals inside 1h merge — is the accepted Chapter session semantic (open-Q1). The >1h gap rule is unchanged, so genuine re-arrivals >1h apart still split.
  - **Validated in a scratch full-rebuild before cutover** (`_v1_rebuild_scratch_new` → `_v2_rebuild_scratch`, compared vs live). **Invariant (verified at v2 per 6.6): chapter count + revenue byte-identical** — EOS 1,638 chapters / **$148,015.43** unchanged, NSC 1,406 / $0, projectagram 74 / $922.89, all `invariant_ok=true`; FULL JOIN recon = 0 only-in-live, 0 only-in-scratch, 0 revenue mismatches. Only 2 EOS chapters flipped to `(direct)` (continuous-session case — the *correct* answer). Path targets: EOS avg 19.2→**7.2**, **median 3**, max 1848→**223**; NSC max 75→**17** (matches the doc's predicted "deduped" exactly); **zero chapters with >10 sessions in a single day** (the definitive proof the intra-visit inflation is gone). The residual avg-7.2 is legitimate — the worst chapters are genuine 40–85-day high-engagement chapters at 2–4 real >1h-separated visits/day; the doc's 3.6 was a consecutive-dedup proxy that wrongly collapses separate-day visits.
  - **6.6 confirmed:** v2 = `chapter_meta LEFT JOIN v1` (`COALESCE(v1.channel_path,'(direct)')`) — it does NOT re-sessionize, so fixing v1 propagates to v2 automatically; the ~+179 EOS v2-over-v1 delta is purely the no-session fallback chapters (untouched).
  - **Cutover:** patched prod `refresh_canonical_v1_snapshot` (forward nightly behavior — the incremental/Fix-1B offset machinery preserved byte-for-byte, only `ordered`+`flagged` changed); backed up live v1/v2 → `chapter_attribution._v1_backup_5_1`/`_v2_backup_5_1` (retained for reversibility); atomically swapped live v1/v2 from the validated scratch; re-ran `refresh_attribution_tables()` (7,166 rows) + `refresh_chapter_purchase_summary_snapshot` per client + the 3 dashboard RPC snapshots (incrementality/contribution/journeys). Full historical rebuild (open-Q3) — the only defensible choice given 1,848-segment paths; the `/chapter/*` dashboard is internal agency-operator-facing so no client-facing retroactive change.
- **7.7 — reporting snapshot reconciliation.** The review's session-7 scan (Incrementality reads v1 for axis + v2 for channel; 6 v1-reading RPCs) predated this session's perf-fix materialization and no longer matched reality. Actual scan: Attribution/Path/Channel/Contribution/Incrementality-channel all already read **v2** (via `chapter_purchase_summary`); Customer Journeys reads `lifecycle_chapters_snapshot` directly (chapter-complete, agrees with v2 count); `matched_lift_bucket_stats` is journey-grain (`journey_resolved_v1`); **`incrementality_axis_metadata` reads neither snapshot — the "disagrees with itself" split is already gone.** Only **two** reporting fns still read v1 directly: `connections_self_recurrence` + `observations_dormant_questions` — both migrated to v2 (column-compatible; v2 is the revenue-complete superset). **Decision locked: v2 is THE canonical reporting snapshot; v1 is an internal build stage (input to v2 + the sessionizer output).** Post-migration scan confirms zero reporting fns read v1 except the builders.
- **Number shifts operators will see (all corrections, not regressions):** Path Patterns `avg_touches` EOS ~26→~7 (median 3); Attribution Models Linear weights shift (a 1,848-direct chapter no longer assigns ~all credit to one channel — First/Last unaffected by construction); Channel Roles opener/mid/closer re-mix (Mid-dominant was an inflation artifact); Cross-Source Influence self-recurrence now counts the complete chapter set. **R4.1 ("path length growing") auto-corrects** — it read the now-fixed v1 and was the doc's flagged "measuring the artifact" rule.
- **App code untouched.** v1 references that remain (recommendation rules R4_1/R4_2/R4_3/R6_2, redirect `segments.ts`, monitoring freshness/daily-digest) are separate subsystems that legitimately read the now-fixed v1 or track it as an existing chain stage. Recommendation-rule v1-vs-v2 choice deferred to the review's separate "Recommendations rule verification" item.
- **Migrations:** `fix_5_1_sessionizer_prod`, `fix_7_7_migrate_v1_readers_to_v2` (+ throwaway `v1_sessionizer_scratch_rebuild_5_1` harness, dropped post-validation). Backups `_v1_backup_5_1`/`_v2_backup_5_1` retained. **Re-verification debt now unlocked** (per the review): Channel Roles, Attribution Linear, Path Patterns Collapsed/Raw, R4.1 all shifted; and the Lift/Incrementality page (blocked on 7.7) is now reviewable against a single canonical.

### EOS identity layer — Group C investigation + 1P collect migration + cookie fixes + cart-token recovery + _shopify_y (July 30–31, 2026)
- **Context:** picked up the tab-by-tab dashboard review's Group C (decisions 7.4 "July linkage regression" + 8.1 "raise anon→known merge coverage"). The review doc's central hypothesis was that identity resolution is broken (bare-UUID keys minted without a type prefix, a July regression collapsing cross-journey linkage 0.97%→0.03%, 16× identity surge). **Investigation refuted the doc's data-layer claims** — a second "verify before building" win (the first was the "EOS blank pages" perf finding, same session).
- **Group C findings (read-only diagnostics, EOS):**
  - **True anon→known stitch rate: 2.37%** (NSC 7.53%). But that's denominator-inflated by 48,063 never-identified anonymous browsers (95% of 50,390 canonicals — normal for ecom). The meaningful metric: **~51% of EOS's ~2,326 KNOWN customers have their pre-identification browsing linked.**
  - **Bare-UUID hypothesis (a) REFUTED:** all 1,380 bare-UUID (no-prefix) keys resolve to an `email_sha256` canonical — they're anonymous browsing that *successfully stitched to email*, not unmergeable orphans. Declining (746 May → 252 Jul), not surging. Re-prefixing them (doc's prescribed fix) would do nothing.
  - **"July regression" NOT reproduced:** cross-journey multi-rate flat-low since March (Mar 0.07 · Apr 0.31 · May 0.07 · Jun 0.09 · Jul 0.05%); no step-change, no 16× surge. The doc's tiny denominators (722 in June) measured some unspecified narrow subset.
  - **Real root cause = anonymous non-persistence (Q3=0):** ZERO anon-only canonicals span >1 journey. The merge engine works (Q2: 1,168 known canonicals span multiple journeys); the gap is that each anonymous *visit* mints a fresh anon_id (cookie/localStorage ITP-capped because the pixel loaded cross-site from ads4good.com), so a returning anonymous visitor never links across visits, and only the identify-moment session merges when they eventually convert.
  - **Conclusion:** the fix is durable first-party identity (the 1P collect migration), NOT identity-graph surgery. Prize: up to ~14,400 human anonymous EOS identities that could start linking.
- **EOS 1P collect migration SHIPPED.** Moved EOS's pixel from 3P (`ads4good.com/api/chapter/collect`) to 1P (`s.eosfabrics.com`) so the anon_id lives in genuinely first-party storage (no ITP 7-day cap; de-risked by Test 1). Operator did: A-record `s.eosfabrics.com → 76.76.21.21` (A-record, NOT CNAME, per Test 1 for ITP durability) + Vercel custom domain + EOS Shopify theme repoint of the pixel `<script src>` + `data-collect-url` + `data-identify-url` from `ads4good.com`/`www.ads4good.com` → `s.eosfabrics.com`. `chapter_config.clients.collect_host` set to `https://s.eosfabrics.com`. Code already supported it (the collect chain `/api/chapter/c → collect → pixelPost` reuses `req.url`, preserving the browser host; `cookieApex` derives `.eosfabrics.com` from `s.eosfabrics.com`). Verified: single clean cookie pair on `.eosfabrics.com`. Rollback = revert the three theme attributes (instant, old collect still live).
- **Two cookie-domain bugs fixed** (surfaced by operator reading the theme): `/api/identify` (commit `10b6c2d`) and `/api/consent` (`070d7f8`) were setting `up_anon`/`up_journey` cookies **host-only (no `Domain`)** — so on a 1P collect host they'd scope to `s.eosfabrics.com` and be invisible to the storefront, fragmenting from the collect-side `.eosfabrics.com` cookie. Both now derive `Domain=.apex` the same way `/api/pixel` does (consent also aligned `SameSite=lax`→`none` on the identity cookies). **Also fixed NSC** (its identify/consent cookies were host-only on `chapter.notsocavalier.com` → now `.notsocavalier.com`). The pixel only *reads* cookies client-side (`readCookieValue`), never writes — so those two server routes were the only host-only sources. Symptom during rollout: a stale duplicate host-only cookie pair from identify/consent calls that fired in the ~2-min window before the fixes deployed; cleared by the operator, no new ones created.
- **Cart-token historical recovery backfill.** Extended the `purchase_cart_token_bridge` concept to link anonymous sessions that shared a persistent Shopify cart cookie (cart cookie persists ~2 weeks independent of our localStorage). Guards, all load-bearing (a sample check caught reused/default tokens spanning 25–105 days that would have been mass false-merges): **span ≤14d + cluster ≤3 + single-known + exclude junk tokens** (one default token spanned 91 anons). Two INSERTs into `chapter_identity.identity_aliases` (ON CONFLICT `(client_key, from_identity_key)` DO NOTHING; canon trigger propagates): **149 anon→known** (`method='cart_token_time_window'`, `reason='cart_token_anon_known_backfill'`, conf 75) run first, then **117 anon→anon** (`reason='cart_token_anon_anon_backfill'`, conf 60). Verified: all 149 now resolve to a known canonical, all 117 to their representative anon. **Honest caveat:** aggregate stitch-% unchanged (2.37→2.36) — it's a canonical-count metric dominated by never-identified browsers, and most recovered anons attached to already-"both" canonicals; the real win is session-level (149 known customers' journeys gain their pre-ID browsing after the nightly chain refresh). Fully reversible via the `reason` tags. Reusable for projectagram (Shopify); NSC is Square (booking-token analog).
- **`_shopify_y` capture SHIPPED** (commit `d42c8cf`, pixel.js). Reads Shopify's durable first-party `_shopify_y` visitor cookie (~1yr) once per session and links it as `identity_key='shopify_visitor:<value>'` — a stable cross-visit anchor that survives even when our anon_id is cleared/capped. Primary cross-visit linker for non-1P Shopify installs (projectagram, which now auto-captures it going forward), belt-and-suspenders for 1P ones (EOS). Self-gating: no-ops where `_shopify_y` is absent (NSC/non-Shopify). sessionStorage-guarded to fire once per session. **`_shopify_y` durability confirmed:** live cookie on a real EOS visit expires **July 2027 (~1yr) — durable**.
- **Daily cart-token stitch cron SHIPPED** (commit `8d1c564`). Turns the July 31 one-time backfill into an automated cadence — the third rung of the fallback ladder for same-person stitching: **(1)** Chapter anon_id (native, now durable on 1P) → **(2)** `shopify_visitor:<_shopify_y>` (durable ~1yr, real-time at identify) → **(3)** cart_token (this cron, catches the case where both stronger signals were cleared/capped but the ~2-week Shopify cart cookie persisted). New SQL fn `chapter_identity.cart_token_stitch_sweep(client, lookback_days=16)` runs the exact guarded anon→known (conf 75) then anon→anon (conf 60) logic from the backfill but over a **rolling 16-day window** (so every 14-day-lifetime token pair is caught daily), `method='cart_token_time_window'`, `reason='cart_token_anon_{known,anon}_sweep'`. Guards unchanged: span≤14d, cluster≤3 anons, single-known(=1)/no-known(=0), `ON CONFLICT (client_key, from_identity_key) DO NOTHING` → **pure fallback + idempotent** (any anon already aliased by a stronger signal or prior sweep is skipped; each anon holds ≤1 outgoing edge). Route `/api/internal/cron/cart-token-stitch` (DATABASE_DIRECT_URL + postgres-js, iterates active `client_secrets`, GChat on failure), scheduled **03:15 UTC** — after `refresh-dashboard-mvs` (03:00) and crucially **before the attribution chain (03:30)** so new aliases propagate through `trg_sync_canon_from_alias` into canon and the same night's chain attributes the merged sessions. Non-Shopify clients (NSC — Square, no cart_token) no-op harmlessly, so the cron iterates every client rather than maintaining a Shopify allowlist. Validated at ship: **0/0 across all 3 clients** (the July 31 all-time backfill already covered the current 16-day window — confirms the fallback + idempotency contract). **No extra paranoia guard** per operator call: worst case of a missed edge is a non-merge, and the cluster/span/single-known guards already bound false-merge risk.
- **Expected 1P coverage (can't measure precisely yet — Q3=0 is what we just fixed):** deterministic cart-token floor shows **~9–15% of engaged (add-to-cart) anonymous EOS visitors were returning** within a cart lifetime and will now link natively (30.5% raw was default-token-inflated; cluster-2–5 = 9%, 2–10 = 15%). Biased up (engaged subset) and down (14-day window vs the 1P fix's ~1-year durability). **TODO: re-run the Q3 / cross-journey-multi metric in ~2–4 weeks** (after `_shopify_y` + durable anon_id accumulate) to measure the actual lift.
- **Queued:** projectagram 1P collect migration + cart-token backfill (Shopify). NSC 1P is `chapter.notsocavalier.com` via **CNAME** — consider flipping to an A-record for full ITP durability (Test 1 proved A-records; CNAME may still be capped).

### Dashboard perf — chapter_purchase_summary materialized behind the stable name (346× on the blocked pages) (July 30–31, 2026)
- **Root cause of the "EOS blank pages" the review doc (5.2/6.7/4.1) misdiagnosed as a channel-map render bug.** Verified: the channel→label map + `CHANNEL_FALLBACK` guards have existed since May; every channel deref is guarded. The pages *render* — Channels/Paths showed an empty-state and Attribution flaked because **`chapter_purchase_summary` runs a 16.7s aggregate over 2.8M `lifecycle_chapters` rows on every call**, the dashboard reads the cold replica through PostgREST's **8s statement_timeout**, so **8 consumer RPCs** errored → wrappers `return []` → "No channel data." (channel_roles / path_combinations / attribution / channel_affinity / channel_performance / correlation / contribution / incrementality).
- **Fix:** new `chapter_reporting.chapter_purchase_summary_snapshot` (window-independent materialization: client_key, canonical, chapter_id, first_ts, boundary_ts, order_id, channel_path, gross/refunded/net_revenue, currency; index `(client_key, boundary_ts)`). **Swapped the implementation behind the stable function name** — renamed the slow original to `chapter_purchase_summary_live` (loader-only), created a new `chapter_purchase_summary` that reads the snapshot via an indexed range scan. **All 8 consumers + the app's direct caller are byte-for-byte unchanged.** Migrations: `chapter_purchase_summary_snapshot_phase1` (table + loader) + `_phase2_flip` (rename + snapshot-backed fn + loader repointed at `_live`). Verified byte-identical (388 rows / $34,595.16, `EXCEPT` both directions = 0). **`channel_roles_overview`: 16,699ms → 48ms (~346×).**
- **Loader:** `refresh_chapter_purchase_summary_snapshot(client)` — full rebuild per client, `_snapshot_runs` telemetry (EOS 18.8s, ~30s total, ~10× under the 300s cron budget; incrementalize only when telemetry says so). Wired into `refresh-derived-snapshots` (04:25 UTC) **before** the Sprint 9 DASHBOARD_RPC_SNAPSHOTS (they read the now-snapshot-backed fn). Added to daily-digest freshness. Commit `b32725a` (cron+digest). **Knock-on:** Lift/Incrementality, Cross-Source Influence, Correlation pages that share the fn are now fast too.
- **Calibration lesson (locked):** the review doc's *code-level* root-cause hypotheses are hypotheses — verify each against the running system before building. Its *data-layer* findings are query-backed and more reliable (though Group C showed even one of those — 7.4/8.1 framing — was substantially overstated).

### Dashboard review cheap-batch cleanups + Test 1 retirement (July 30–31, 2026)
- **Test 1 DEFINITIVE at day 10** (cookie present 3 days past ITP's 7-day cap on real Safari, exact prod config: A-record, HttpOnly:false, server-set, Max-Age=1yr). `sctest` rig retired: route deleted, Vercel domain + DNS removed. `HttpOnly:false` ships as-is (already the config; no dual-cookie work).
- **7.1** — hardcoded "EOS data depth caveat" on Lagged Impact rendered for *every* client (cross-tenant copy leak). Made client-neutral (also dropped the wrong-for-NSC "April 2026 / ~8 weeks"). Dashboard-wide grep audit: only that one user-facing leak; other `eos_fabrics` hits are server-component default fallbacks.
- **Attribution Models 6.1/6.2/6.3 + 5.3-adjacent** ([AttributionClient.tsx](src/app/chapter/(authed)/attribution/AttributionClient.tsx)): removed the Custom J-shape pill + "New custom model" (not built; scaffolding preserved); each model states its allocation rule on-page (Linear explicitly covers the repeat-touch case, matching the per-touch SQL); **revenue↔conversions toggle** (label from `boundary_event_name` — "Orders" EOS, "Bookings" NSC; shares recompute from `*_orders` vs `*_revenue`; Linear counts fractional with a note); **unattributed-revenue caption** ($35,938 vs $34,678 gap = the "(unknown)" bucket, so the allocation reconciles to the headline). Commit `9e6291a`.
- **Path Patterns 5.4** ([PathsClient.tsx](src/app/chapter/(authed)/paths/PathsClient.tsx)): a NEW row never shows a numeric move; a combo present in a *usable* prior is never NEW; near-empty priors (<3 combos or <30 chapters, e.g. NSC's 485-of-487 direct) suppress NEW wholesale; tiny-base combos show absolute delta ("+48") not a misleading "+2400%". Follow-up (`74474c4`): low-sample rows (n<5) show "—" for movement (no noise claim), zero delta renders "—" not "+0".
- **Sample-size floor** (the review's most-deferred item): non-destructive "low sample" chip on Channel Roles cards (<20 chapters) + Path combos (<5). Commit `9e6291a`.
- All client-side (+ one server prop). The perf-fix cron (`b32725a`) is the only server/DB piece; the rest deploy freely.

### Self-serve signup email-bombing — investigation, containment, 3-layer hardening + monitoring (July 30, 2026)
- **Trigger:** operator opened `/internal` links + prompts and saw 2 unrecognized clients — `afjyccmwexwaozek_01` + `jvuhsqvxemukffgxkhnluol_01`. Emergency investigation.
- **Diagnosis — signup-form email bombing.** Both were junk self-serve tenants auto-provisioned by the OPEN signup flow (`/chapter/signup` → `/api/chapter-auth/signup` → `provision_self_serve_tenant`; the gibberish client keys are just the slugifier turning gibberish company names into `_01` slugs). Both completed magic-link auth (real `user_id`s → working victim inboxes) but had gibberish name/phone/company, zero prompts, zero links, zero pixel events. The 2 tenants were the tip: `chapter_config.pending_signups` held **40 rows, 40 distinct emails (distinct even after Gmail-dot normalization)** — every one a different real victim email (Gmail dot-variants, hotmail, yahoo, web.de, orange.fr, `.org` domains) with gibberish other fields. Escalating volume: 3 (Jul 26) → 4 → 7 → 17 (Jul 29) → 9 (Jul 30). The all-distinct-emails signature rules out account-farming (which reuses controlled inboxes) — this is the open endpoint being used as a free "send a branded magic-link email to any address" weapon. Each submission fired a Supabase→Resend email at the victim. Only 2 recipients clicked (→ the 2 tenants); the other 38 just got spammed.
- **Root cause — zero bot protection on signup.** The endpoint had no rate limit, no CAPTCHA, no honeypot, no domain screening; it called `signInWithOtp` for any submitted email. The sibling `/api/chapter-auth/magic-link` is SAFE (allowlist-gated — only emails pre-existing `chapter_config.users`), so signup was the entire attack surface.
- **Biggest risk was deliverability, not the 2 junk rows.** 40 unsolicited branded emails from ads4good.com's Resend domain invite spam complaints + spamtrap hits that degrade sender reputation → can push ALL Chapter transactional email (client magic links, inquiry replies, offer emails, Remind Me) toward spam. Actual damage was tiny — Resend showed **1 complaint + 2 bounces** — negligible, decaying now that the bombing is stopped. No breach of victim data (attacker-supplied emails; no notification obligation). Decision: no proactive "sorry" email to the 40 (a second unsolicited send compounds the exact problem + reads as phishing + risks more complaints); handle any replies one-off.
- **Containment (DB):** revoked the 2 junk tenants' `chapter_config.users` rows (`revoked_at`), purged all 40 `pending_signups`. Later, per operator, **hard-deleted both tenants** — `chapter_config.{client_secrets, users, clients}` (only 3 tables held rows; `allowed_email_domains` + `chapter_inquiries.threads` FK'd to clients but were empty). 2 inert Supabase `auth.users` rows left (no `chapter_config.users` row = no access; purge via Dashboard → Auth → Users if desired).
- **Hardening — 3-layer defense on signup** (mirrors the June 19 pixel-submit pattern), commit `f31eb08` on `main`:
  1. **Honeypot** (`hp_field`) — hidden input; filled → silent fake-success (`ok:true`, no email sent) so bots don't learn.
  2. **Cloudflare Turnstile CAPTCHA** (`turnstile_token`) — primary control. New helper [src/app/lib/auth/turnstile.ts](src/app/lib/auth/turnstile.ts). **Fail-open until `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` is set** (and on Cloudflare network error) so signup never breaks before keys are provisioned; honeypot + rate-limit are the backstop in that window. Widget rendered explicitly in [signup/page.tsx](src/app/chapter/signup/page.tsx) only when `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` is set; submit disabled until solved. Turnstile is free with no request cap.
  3. **Per-IP rate limit** (5/hr, in-memory per instance) — runs BEFORE the Turnstile network call so a flood hits the cheap check first and doesn't burn siteverify quota.
  - Every rejection logs to `chapter_audit.api_auth_attempts` (`endpoint='/api/chapter-auth/signup'`, `client_key='self_serve_signup'`, reasons `honeypot_filled` / `turnstile_*` / `rate_limited`). Files: [signup/route.ts](src/app/api/chapter-auth/signup/route.ts), [turnstile.ts](src/app/lib/auth/turnstile.ts), [signup/page.tsx](src/app/chapter/signup/page.tsx).
  - **Env vars (Production only, confirmed set):** `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` + `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY`. Production-only (not Preview) so random Vercel preview hostnames don't need allowlisting in the Cloudflare widget — previews skip CAPTCHA via fail-open and stay usable. `NEXT_PUBLIC_` is build-time inlined → the widget appears on builds produced after the vars exist.
  - **Rejected: gibberish name/company validation.** No reliable "is this gibberish" test; company/personal names are the worst-case input (invented brands like `Tronix3D`, non-English names, single-word companies all misfire). Turnstile already stops the bot before submit, so name validation is redundant with the control that works, and blocking a real customer is worse than letting a stopped bot's gibberish through. If ever wanted, do advisory-only (annotate the notification, never block).
- **Monitoring — closed the detection gap** (commit `0601ff1` on `main`):
  - **`prompt-attack-alert` cron** (15-min) was scoped `.eq(endpoint, identity-prompt-email)` — signup was invisible to it. Broadened to `WATCHED_ENDPOINTS` (identity-prompt-email + signup), added `turnstile_*` as attack-shaped (prefix match via `isAttackReason`), added a by-endpoint breakdown to the alert. Fires ≥ threshold (default 10) in 15 min to `CHAPTER_SECURITY_GCHAT_WEBHOOK_URL`.
  - **`daily-digest` cron** (14:00 UTC) gained a "Self-serve signup abuse (24h)" section — blocked signup attempts grouped by `failure_reason`. This is the coverage for the **low-and-slow cadence** (~1-2/hr, like this incident) that stays under the 15-min burst threshold.
  - **Real-time per-tenant ping** already existed (commit `4b73b8b`, same day): every `provisionSelfServeTenant` pings `CHAPTER_SIGNUPS_GCHAT_WEBHOOK_URL` (fallback `CHAPTER_GCHAT_WEBHOOK_URL`). Best "a junk tenant just appeared" signal — fires regardless of rate. All three webhook env vars confirmed set.
- **Deploy state:** signup hardening (`f31eb08`) + monitoring (`0601ff1`) both pushed to `main`/production. This CLAUDE.md entry is a local edit only (not committed — batched with future building per operator).
- **Prior legit self-serve tenants unaffected:** `k_man` (July 21) is the only real self-serve tenant and was untouched.

### MI v2 Phase 6b — Remind Me admin form + pixel renderer SHIPPED (July 28, 2026 late-late evening)
- **User-visible now:** the "Phase 6 · not shipped" placeholder in `/internal/identity-prompts/[clientKey]` PromptForm is gone. Remind Me is a real, authorable preset — operator picks target (product / variant) + trigger type (back_in_stock / price_below with threshold) + optional per-prompt max_notifications override. Visitors clicking a Remind Me prompt trigger see a real modal with email capture that creates a subscription row.
- **New `RemindMeBuilder.tsx`** (~420 lines) mirrors the MakeAnOfferBuilder pattern: content_blocks composer (headline + body for the subscribe modal), target picker (product | variant with product_id / variant_id / product_name / variant_name / product_url / list_price display fields), trigger picker (back_in_stock | price_below with $-threshold input), notification cap input (default 3, hard cap 10). All operator-facing, uses the same design language as the other MI v2 builders.
- **PromptForm.tsx wiring:** `PRESET_OPTIONS` flipped `remind_me` from `available: false` → `available: true`. New state `remindMeConfig` hydrates from `container_jsonb.remind_me` on edit + defaults to `{ target: {type: 'product', product_id: ''}, trigger: {type: 'back_in_stock'} }` on create. Submit routing writes `content_blocks_jsonb` + `container_jsonb: { type: 'modal', remind_me: { target, trigger, max_notifications } }` — nested inside container_jsonb to reuse the existing composable slot instead of a new DB column. `form_fields_jsonb` set to `[]` for remind_me submits (subscription runtime doesn't use it). Placeholder card replaced with real Section-wrapped RemindMeBuilder inline + a helper note that references the Phase 6c cron endpoint + email templates. Preview column placeholder message updated to explain the preview limitation honestly (subscribe → cron-detects-transition → notification is a server-side flow that can't be shown in a static preview).
- **PromptFormInput.container_jsonb type extended** (in `_actions.ts` + `ExistingPrompt`) with an optional `remind_me: { target, trigger, max_notifications? }` field. Zero migration — jsonb slot, additive.
- **Pixel renderer `chapterRenderPromptRemindMe`** (~230 lines) added to `pixel.js`. Dispatch: `preset_type='remind_me'` → this renderer. Reads `container_jsonb.remind_me` for target + trigger + max_notifications, renders modal with (a) operator content_blocks or a sensible default headline based on trigger type ("Notify me when the price drops" vs "Notify me when it's back in stock"), (b) product summary + trigger-appropriate clause ("We'll email you the moment X is back in stock" vs "We'll email you when X drops below $Y"), (c) email input + honeypot + submit button. On submit: hashes email → `api.identify(email_sha256:X)` for identity graph → POSTs raw email + target + trigger + max_notifications to `/api/chapter/subscription-create`. Success state distinguishes new-subscription vs already-subscribed (endpoint returns `created: false, reason: 'already_subscribed'` for the idempotent duplicate case).
- **New `chapterPostSubscriptionCreate` helper** in pixel.js — same shape as `chapterPostOfferSubmit`. HMAC session_token + honeypot flow inherited from the shared submit pattern.
- **What Phase 6c needs to add next session:**
  1. `/api/internal/cron/evaluate-subscriptions` — hourly Vercel cron. Iterates active subs → `selectAdapter(client_key).getProduct(product_id)` → matches trigger (back_in_stock: transition from prior in_stock=false to true; price_below: current price < threshold AND either first eval or transition from ≥ threshold to below) → sends `back_in_stock` or `price_drop` template via `sendEmail()` → increments notification_count → updates last_evaluated_at + last_notified_at. When notification_count reaches max_notifications → sets `canceled_at + cancel_reason='max_notifications_reached'`.
  2. Auto-cancel-on-purchase hook in `/api/purchase` — after successful purchase insert, look up active subs for the purchasing `email_sha256` canonical identity → mark `canceled_at + cancel_reason='purchased'`. Prevents "you just bought it, why are you emailing me about restock" complaints.
  3. Admin subscriptions view at `/internal/identity-prompts/[clientKey]/subscriptions` — table of active + canceled subs with filter, showing prompt_slug + target + trigger + last_notified_at + notification_count + cancel reason. Read-only for now; operator can INSERT/UPDATE via SQL if needed.
  4. E2E test walkthrough documented in the ship commit.
- **Commit:** `TBD` (this session).

### MI v2 Phase 6a — Remind Me foundation SHIPPED (July 28, 2026 late evening)
- **Foundation-only ship** — the runtime plumbing that Phase 6b (UI + pixel) and Phase 6c (cron + auto-cancel + admin view + E2E test) will bolt onto. User-facing behavior unchanged tonight; Remind Me still shows as "Phase 6 · not shipped" placeholder in PromptForm.
- **`chapter_engagement.subscriptions` table was already fully designed** (MI v2 Phase 1, June 29): `target_resource_jsonb` (target type + id / variant_id / list_price), `trigger_condition_jsonb` (type: back_in_stock / price_below with optional threshold), `notify_channels text[]` (email today, SMS in v2.1), `max_notifications integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 3` cap so operators can't misconfigure spammy prompts, `last_evaluated_at + last_notified_at + notification_count` for cron bookkeeping, `canceled_at + cancel_reason` for auto-cancel-on-purchase (Phase 6c). Ready to use as-is, no schema migration.
- **New `chapter_config.mock_products` table + 2 seed rows for adsforgood_prod:** `remind-me-hoodie` starts out-of-stock (`in_stock=false, inventory_count=0`), `remind-me-jacket` starts at $199 with 12 in stock. Testing scenarios: flip `in_stock=true` via SQL → the Phase 6c cron detects the delta on next tick → visitor gets the back-in-stock email. Same for `price` — drop it and get the price_drop email. `shopify-mock.ts` extended to READ from this DB table first, falling back to the hardcoded `MOCK_PRODUCTS` registry for backward-compat with existing Make an Offer demo prompts.
- **`PlatformAdapter.ProductInfo` extended** with optional `inventory_count?: number` (Shopify: yes, Square: sometimes, custom: usually null). The Remind Me evaluator will use `in_stock` (boolean) for `back_in_stock` triggers and `price` (already existed) for `price_below` triggers; `inventory_count` is for future features that need a threshold like "notify me when > 10 back in stock."
- **`/api/chapter/subscription-create` endpoint (271 lines)** — mirrors `/api/chapter/offer-submit` shape. 3-layer defenses (honeypot + HMAC session token + 10/hr per-IP rate limit), audit logging on rejects, `preset_type='remind_me'` gate. Validates target + trigger shape (`back_in_stock` allows no threshold; `price_below` requires a positive numeric threshold). **Idempotent** — if the visitor already has an active subscription for the same `(identity_key, target_resource_jsonb, trigger_condition_jsonb)`, returns the existing subscription_id + `created: false, reason: 'already_subscribed'` instead of creating a duplicate. Fire-and-forget CRM adapter mirror at end (adsforgood_prod → crm.prospects) with subscription context in `metadata.responses`. Inserts into `chapter_engagement.subscriptions` with `notify_channels: ['email']` (Phase 6a: email only).
- **2 email templates seeded for adsforgood_prod:** `back_in_stock` (subject `"{{product_name}} is back in stock"` + body with `{{product_url}}` merge token + one-shot note "you won't get another back-in-stock email for the same product until it goes out and comes back again") and `price_drop` (subject `"{{product_name}} just dropped in price"` + body with `{{current_price}}` + `{{target_price}}` + `{{product_url}}` + "we'll stop watching this one now"). Chapter's email-send infra (Phase 3) resolves outbound sends against `chapter_config.email_templates` on (client_key, template_type).
- **What Phase 6b will add next session:** `RemindMeBuilder.tsx` admin form (target picker for product/variant + trigger type picker + threshold input for price_below), PromptForm wiring (remove the "Phase 6 · not shipped" placeholder tile, add state + submit routing), `PromptPreview` extension (or `PreviewPlaceholder` for now), pixel renderer `chapterRenderPromptRemindMe` (modal with email capture + trigger picker + product summary).
- **What Phase 6c will add session after:** `/api/internal/cron/evaluate-subscriptions` (hourly Vercel cron; iterates active subs, calls `selectAdapter(client_key).getProduct(product_id)`, evaluates trigger match, sends email via `sendEmail()`, updates `last_evaluated_at / last_notified_at / notification_count`), auto-cancel-on-purchase hook in `/api/purchase` (looks up active subs for the purchasing identity + marks canceled with reason='purchased'), admin subscriptions view at `/internal/identity-prompts/[clientKey]/subscriptions`, plus the E2E test walkthrough (subscribe → SQL-flip mock state → curl cron → email lands → auto-cancel via synthetic purchase).
- **Real Shopify support** — `shopify.ts` currently returns null for `getProduct` (Make an Offer's demo path uses the mock). When first real Shopify client onboards, the real GraphQL implementation is a standard `productVariant(id: $id) { inventoryQuantity price }` query — ~15 lines. Adapter selection via `PLATFORM_ADAPTER_MODE=mock` env override or credentials-present-detection handles the swap without engine changes.

### Test 1 ratification + 1P infra schema prep + Make an Offer CRM intercept (July 28, 2026 evening)
- **HttpOnly cookie decision locked: ship HttpOnly:false as-is.** Test 1 (A-record cookie durability on real Safari, HttpOnly:false explicitly) definitively passed at day 8 — cookie present 7d18h past ITP's documented 7-day cap threshold. No delayed sweep, no silent expiry rewrite. Chapter's actual pixel API config (`Max-Age=31536000, HttpOnly:false, Secure, SameSite=None`) is safe to keep unchanged on the 1P collect migration. No dual-cookie complexity needed (which would have added a HttpOnly primary + JS-readable pointer variant if Safari had capped). Zero code change — this IS how the pixel is configured today. Recording the decision so we don't relitigate.
- **`chapter_config.clients` schema additions (migration `add_1p_infra_host_columns_to_clients`).** 3 new nullable text columns, no defaults, no behavior change — pure prep for the EOS + projectagram 1P collect migration (1P infra top-3 #2, now unblocked by Test 1). Semantics per column:
  - `links_host` — per-client 1P subdomain for wrapped redirects (e.g. `go.eosfabrics.com`). Supersedes existing `redirect_host` (used by NSC's `chapter.notsocavalier.com` setup) once populated per client.
  - `collect_host` — per-client 1P subdomain for pixel collect (e.g. `s.eosfabrics.com`). Pixel `getApiOrigin()` keeps auto-deriving from script src until this is explicitly set.
  - `legacy_host` — old hostname preserved during a migration (e.g. `chapter.notsocavalier.com` after NSC moves off it). Route resolver reads this first for backward-compat resolution.
  Existing tenants stay at NULL for all three; every existing behavior unchanged. When a client opts into 1P collect, we populate the columns + flip resolver reads to prefer them over the legacy `redirect_host` + pixel auto-derive.
- **Make an Offer CRM intercept.** `/api/chapter/offer-submit` now mirrors the bid submission to `crm.prospects` via the same fire-and-forget `selectCrmAdapter(clientKey).upsertLead(...)` pattern as `/api/chapter/lead`. Includes `bid_amount / decision / counter_amount` in the responses metadata so the operator sees what was offered + how the state machine decided (auto_accept / counter / decline / review). **No Make an Offer prompt is live on any real customer-facing page today** (Phase 5 is fully built + working in `/internal/demo`, but no real client has one configured) — this is preventive plumbing so it works out-of-the-box when a real Shopify sales conversation activates it. ~30 lines added; zero-risk for existing traffic since offer-submit endpoint is only called from Make an Offer prompts, of which zero fire on public pages today.
- **Commits:** migration `add_1p_infra_host_columns_to_clients` applied via MCP; code + this CLAUDE.md update ship together.

### CRM adapter Path A — Phase 2: single-intercept consolidation at /api/chapter/lead (July 28, 2026 midday)
- **Pivot from the multi-endpoint plan.** Original plan was to add `_crm_lead` payload from pixel + wire CRM adapter into `/api/identify` + `/api/chapter/prompt-response` + gate via a new `crm_enabled` flag in the prompts fetch response. Deep-read of the pixel showed `chapterPostLead` was already firing on both Email Exchange (v1 + v1.5 paths) AND Custom Form (composable path), sending raw email + phone to `/api/chapter/lead` — an endpoint built for the self-serve Leads view but that runs unconditionally for every tenant. All the plumbing I was about to add already existed for a different reason. Reverted the earlier speculative changes, wired the CRM adapter into this one central endpoint. Much cleaner ship.
- **What ships:** in `/api/chapter/lead`, after the successful `chapter_engagement.captured_leads` insert, fire-and-forget `selectCrmAdapter(clientKey).upsertLead({ email, phone, prompt_slug, responses, page_url, ip_country })`. Null-provider → no-op. `chapter_internal` adapter runs the SELECT-then-INSERT-or-UPDATE against `crm.prospects` from the July-28-morning ship (unchanged). Only fires when `email` is present (crm.prospects has no phone-alone dedup key; phone-only leads land in captured_leads but don't mirror). Consent-declined leads (visitor picked "No" on yes/no consent) explicitly skipped — still audited via captured_leads but not pushed to marketing-oriented systems.

Both endpoints fire in the same submit flow for Email Exchange with email post-submit — keeping the call in both would double-append the same submission to `metadata.submissions[]` on the prospect. Comment updated in the file so future engineers know why the CRM dispatch lives in `/api/chapter/lead` and not here.

Phone-only submissions land in `chapter_engagement.captured_leads` (for the self-serve Leads view) but don't mirror to CRM until phone-dedup logic exists.
- **What's still NOT touched by this ship:** `/api/chapter/offer-submit` (Make an Offer bid submissions) receives raw email as `recipient_email` but doesn't yet mirror to CRM — same pattern applies + ~10-line follow-up when requested; not shipped tonight since the ask was "Phone and form."
- **Zero pixel changes.** Non-adsforgood tenants (EOS / projectagram / NSC + all future clients with `crm_provider=null`) have byte-identical behavior — the pixel + lead endpoint fire the same as before; adapter returns null; no CRM writes. Chapter's "we don't touch raw email" contract holds by default.
